## Document 031: 2026年4-6月期GDP予測（1次速報） ～前期比年率＋1.6％と予想。イラン情勢悪化のなかでも景気は崩れず～

Economic Indicators

2026 年 7 月31 日

1/3 
2026 年 4-6 月期 GDP 予測（1 次速報） 
～前期比年率＋1.6％と予想。イラン情勢悪化のなかでも景気は崩れず～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

個人消費を牽引役に、イラン情勢悪化の中でも高い成長を予想 
８月17 日に公表される2026 年４-６月期の実質ＧＤＰ成長率を前期比年率＋1.6％（前期比＋
0.4％）と予測する。３四半期連続のプラス成長になるだろう1。 
今期の牽引役は個人消費になることが予想される。名目賃金の高い伸びが続くなか、物価上昇
率が鈍化したことで実質賃金が前年比で増加に転じ、家計の購買力が徐々に回復している。この
ことが、足元の個人消費が想定以上に底堅く推移した背景にあるとみられる。自動車やエアコン
需要の増加については一時的な面も含まれているため一定程度割り引いて見る必要があるもの
の、４-６月期の個人消費は総じて持ち直し方向にあった。 
４-６月期の景気を巡っては、以前は、イラン情勢の悪化に伴う輸入価格の上昇や供給制約を背
景に、マイナス成長に陥るとの見方もあった。しかし、国家備蓄の放出や代替調達の進展によっ
て深刻な供給制約が回避されたことに加え、ＡＩ関連需要の強まりなどもあり、景気は想定以上
の底堅さを示している。イラン情勢が悪化するなかでも景気回復の流れが崩れず、日本経済が一
定の耐久力を保った点は前向きに評価できる。

輸入減少の解釈に注意 
 ４-６月期の外需寄与度は前期比年率＋1.5％Ptになると予想している。輸出が小幅に減少する
一方、輸入が前期比▲2.5％と大きく減少するとみられるためである。一見、このことが４-６月
期の成長率上振れをもたらしたようにも見える。 
もっとも、この輸入の下振れは国内需要の減少が主因ではなく、中東からの資源輸入が激減し
たことによってもたらされた面が大きい。この輸入減による国内供給の不足は原油の国家備蓄の
放出によって一部賄われており、それは公的在庫変動の減少としてあらわれる。推計には大きな
不確実性があるため幅を持ってみる必要があるが、４-６月期の公的在庫変動の寄与度は前期比▲
0.2％Pt（前期比年率▲0.9％Pt）と予想している。

1 7 月31 日時点で入手可能な経済指標を元に作成している。国際収支や家計調査等、今後公表される経済指標の結果を
踏まえて予測値を修正する可能性がある。

### Page 2

2/3 
つまり、輸入減少による外需寄与度の押し上げの一定部分は、公的在庫の取り崩しによって相
殺される構図である。４-６月期の成長率を評価する際には、外需寄与度の表面的な大きさだけで
なく、その背景にある輸入減少と公的在庫の取り崩しを併せてみる必要がある。

需要項目別の動向 
個人消費は前期比＋0.3％と増加を予想する。名
目賃金が高い伸びを続けるなか、物価上昇率が鈍
化したことで、実質賃金は前年比でプラスに転じ
ている。物価高によって抑制されてきた家計の購
買力が徐々に回復し、そのことが消費の底堅さに
つながっているとみられる。 
また、26 年３月末に自動車税および軽自動車税
の環境性能割が廃止されたことで自動車販売が増
加した。加えて、27 年４月からの省エネ性能の新
基準導入を前にした駆け込み需要によりエアコン
販売が伸びるなど、耐久財消費が大きく増えたと
みられる。 
エアコンや自動車販売の増加は持続性に乏しい
ため、ある程度割り引いて見る必要がある。ただ
し、耐久財以外のサービス消費なども４-６月期は
底堅く推移した模様であり、個人消費全体として
は持ち直しの動きが強まったと評価してよいだろ
う。 
設備投資は前期比▲0.4％と、２四半期連続の減少を予想する。イラン情勢の悪化を受けて一時
的に調達不安や物流面の混乱が生じたことに加え、先行き不透明感の強まりから、企業が一部の
投資を手控えた可能性がある。 
もっとも、企業収益は高水準を維持しており、景況感も良好な水準で推移している。人手不足
に対応するための省力化投資やデジタル関連投資への需要も引き続き強く、企業の設備投資は引
き続き強いままである。設備投資の基調が崩れたと判断する必要はなく、先行きを悲観する必要
もないだろう。 
輸出は前期比▲0.3％と小幅減を予想する。イラン情勢の悪化に伴う混乱を受け、中東向けの自
動車輸出が急減したことなどが押し下げ要因となった。一方、４-６月期には、大口要因によって
研究開発サービスの受取が急増し、サービス輸出が大きく押し上げられたとみられる。この増加
は一時的な性格が強く、輸出全体の基調を判断する際には割り引いてみる必要がある。財輸出に
限れば、前期比▲1.6％の減少が予想される。 
輸入は前期比▲2.5％と大幅な減少が予想される。イラン情勢の悪化により中東からの輸入が激

ただし、この外需寄与度のプラスは、輸出の増加によるものではなく、資源輸入が困難だった

的在庫変動は前期比▲0.2％ポイント、前期比年率では▲0.9％ポイントのマイナス寄与になると
見込まれる。

戰火影響料屬短期，無礙市場中期走勢
2026 年 4 月
美國對伊朗展開襲擊所引發的中東戰火延續至今，拖累 3 月截至 27 日的全球金融股市表現，
市場在多重變數交織下動能顯著受壓，主要股市指數單月報酬全盤皆墨。不過，在能源類股大漲的支
撐下，S&P 500 指數表現相對其他股市穩定。日本股市雖亦出現獲利了結壓力，但受惠於高市政府
「負責且積極的財政政策」 及日圓匯率弱勢有利出口 ， 今年以來 MSCI 日本指數的表現僅次於拉丁美
洲股市。另一方面，受油價高漲帶動通膨預期、外資持續撤出且經濟放緩所累，印度股市出現賣壓，

MSCI 印度指數不論在 3 月或是今年至今表現都是敬陪末座。
全球主要債市 3 月報酬亦全數下跌。在所有券種之中 ， 以新興當地債下跌 5.21% 表現最為疲弱 ，
除了油價引發市場對於進口原油的新興國家前景的擔憂外 ，高漲的美元指數也使得當地貨幣計價新興
債表現呈壓。 全球主權債同樣因為油價高漲推升通膨疑慮 ，投資人對於各國央行貨幣政策或因而轉為
更為鷹派高度警戒，使其表現差強人意。
自去年川普關稅「解放日」後美股跌至低點後開始走揚，各國股市與主要指數持續上揚，其中又
以金礦指數、南韓股市、費半指數、日本股市表現最佳。然而，自中東戰事 2/28 爆發後，以指數修
正幅度來看，跌幅最大的市場分別為金礦指數、南韓股市、日本股市。唯有費半指數則在 AI 需求暢
旺的加持下成為例外。由此可見，本波市場的回跌，基本上為「漲多跌多」的修正效應。
就基本面來看，2026 年美股企業獲利增長主軸仍在 AI， 由於2026 年仍在 AI 基礎建設期擴張間 ，
AI 終端需求仍是科技龍頭企業，故所帶動的獲利增長料不致受油價影響，今年單季盈餘增長可望維

持 2 位數水準。自中東戰事以來，彭博對於 MSCI 美國指數 Q1 及 Q2 的 EPS 預期不跌反漲，顯示市
場對於美國企業的獲利前景仍極具信心。同時，目前的資訊科技類股的預期 P/E 比與過去 10 年的均
值相較仍過有遭到低估之嫌，也預示科技類股相較於其他類股仍有進一步向上的動能。
再者，儘管近期美國公債殖利率因市場的通膨疑慮而上揚 ， 但目前殖利率遠高於Fed 長期通膨目
標，投資人仍可以資產配置角度對於債券作適當之配置。考量短期殖利率受市場對 Fed 的利率政策
預期變動影響較大，故在年期與券種的選擇上可以 7~15 年的中長期美國公債或投資級債為主。

美國股市：標普 500 企業盈餘增長加速，私募信貸贖回潮不致衝擊美國經濟
美國第四季 GDP 季增年率修正值 0.7%，較初值 1.4%有所調降，主要反映實質個人消費季比降
至 0.7%。第四季 GDP 數據整體仍支持美國經濟穩健。但第一季受通膨(關稅與伊朗危機)與高利率影
響，消費行為呈現「降級消費」特徵，非必需性支出收縮，經濟呈現倚賴「高收入族群支撐消費」的
K 型分化格局。由於中東戰火推高能源價格，美國民眾擔心生活成本壓力惡化，美國密西根大學3 月
消費者信心指數降至 55.5，較 2 月終值 56.6 下降 1.1，觸及今年以來最低紀錄，反映中東危機與生

活成本壓力削弱民眾樂觀情緒。
2 月非農就業人數淨減少 9.2 萬個，低於彭博預期的增加 1.3 萬，失業率躍升至 4.4%，失業率雖
短期上揚、但就長期角度仍在低檔。追究原因數據主要是受到罷工和天氣等短期因素干擾，導致醫療
健保、建築業、零售業和休閒酒店等產業表現不佳，但整體勞動市場狀態溫和，從請領就業金人數等
數據看來就業市場尚未出現顯著惡化跡象。市場先前擔憂 AI 取代人力，目前數據看來企業僅是招募
保守，後續可觀察勞動市場降溫是否反映在薪資成長，若薪資壓力下降有助於抑制服務業通膨，除直
接改善企業成本結構外，也將使聯準會擔憂的服務業通膨出現放緩。
預估第一季標普 500 企業餘年增仍可達到 11.3%，且在第二季顯著拉升至近兩成水準，科技板
塊受惠於 AI 仍是帶動美股企業獲利增長主軸。大型科技巨擘透過AI 優化流程，開始對利潤率產生正
面影響。然而，此一效益目前高度集中於資本與技術優勢明顯的科技龍頭企業，中小企業與非科技產
業尚未顯著受益。因此，企業獲利將呈現明顯分化格局，而尚未受惠於 AI 的非科技業者，將會在未

來幾年逐步提高使用 AI 程度，傳統企業大規模導入 AI 模型與使用算力，將可成為 AI 龍頭下一波業
績成長動能。
近期美國私募信貸市場爆發贖回潮，包含貝萊德、阿波羅、藍鷹(Blue Owl)等巨頭均遭投資人要
求提款，甚至限制贖回以防止流動性枯竭。此波動盪主因投資人擔憂 AI 技術衝擊軟體業導致違約風
險上升，以及對高收益資產的獲利了結。研判此類事件雖造成金融股拉回，但引發總體系統性危機的
可能性極低。
目前標普 500 本益比估計在 22.84 倍、股價淨值比 4.95 倍，短期評價偏高，面對伊朗戰事的干
擾，預料股市震盪加劇，但若市場拉回，基於美國在 AI 產業具備絕對優勢，仍建議以長線角度布局。

歐洲股市：歐元區經濟表現穩健，央行面對能源危機採取警戒態度
歐元區第四季 GDP 季增率自初值的 0.3%下修至 0.2%，年增則下修至 1.2%，二者皆低於市場預
期。歐元區第四季經濟動能略微放緩，成長主要由內需帶動。家庭消費在穩健的勞動力市場與薪資增
長支撐下，季增率上升至 0.2%，固定資本形成雖由高點回落，但仍維持正成長，外部需求疲軟導致
出口季減，加上企業去庫存，顯示內需支撐正與貿易逆風強烈拉鋸。
三月份歐元區經濟活動連續 15 個月處於擴張區間，其中製造業表現強勁，歐元區製造業PMI 由
50.8 上升至 51.4，創 45 個月高點，受新訂單增長支撐，惟出口訂單連續第 49 個月低於榮枯線，外

需疲軟仍未改善。服務業受中東戰爭引發的不確定性與能源成本飆升衝擊，驟降至 50.1，放緩至 10
個月低點。預料南歐以觀光業為主國家將面對能源成本衝擊，而外銷為主的如德國、法國將迎來能源
與運輸成本飆升的雙重打擊。歐盟決定延後提交原定 4/15 提出將俄羅斯石油進口永久禁令入法的提
案，自 2022 年俄羅斯全面入侵烏克蘭以來，歐盟已大幅降低對俄能源依賴，是否因為伊朗戰爭調整
制裁內容將是未來關注重點。
歐洲央行在 3 月利率決策會議決定利率不變、但態度轉鷹。歐洲央行總裁拉加德表示，若通膨出
現「顯著但不過於持久」的超標情況，可能進行「適度的政策調整」，以確保通膨預期維持穩定。她
強調 ， 若對通膨偏離目標完全不作反應 ， 恐影響政策溝通與市場信心 ， 使外界難以理解央行反應機制 。
從利率期貨觀察 ， 市場預期歐洲央行與英國央行都有機會在第二季升息1 碼 ， 不排除未來一年升息到
2 碼，顯示歐洲央行與英國央行面對能源危機採取警戒態度。
在地緣風險常態化背景下，歐洲各國持續提高國防支出，德國政府規劃在2026 年投入 380 億歐

元於基礎建設、氣候保護專項基金、國防及數位化，加上 AI 浪潮帶動企業投資，投資者原本對歐洲
經濟抱持樂觀態度，然而油價大漲大幅扭轉市場樂觀預期。 考量市場目前悲觀原因仍是地緣政治風
險，假設美伊衝突能在第二季落幕，對歐股企業獲利影響有限，歐洲經濟將在下半年重回成長軌道。
泛歐 600 指數目前本益比為 16.14，故價淨值比為 2.27。就評價角度相對合理，雖然年初以來歐
股的樂觀情緒因為伊朗戰爭快速扭轉，但未來若油價回落，或是歐洲重新考慮向俄羅斯購買能源，都
能夠化解歐洲面臨的能源困境，故仍可視為長線佈局標的。

日本股市：高市政府加大戰略產業投資，政府補貼壓抑油價衝擊
日本受惠於企業強勁支出 ，2025 年第四季 GDP 年化季增率為 1.3%， 優於原先預估的0.2%。2026
年不僅企業資本支出動能持續 ， 在高市早苗出任首相後 ， 政府選定支持包含實體人工智慧 、 再生醫學 、
量子運算與海洋無人機等 61 個項目，目標在 2040 年前取得全球 AI 機器人逾 30%的市占率，國產
晶片銷售額也要在同年衝上 40 兆日圓。高市希望藉由政府與民間的合作推動投資，強化發展包含人
工智慧、半導體、宇宙及航太等 17 大重點產業。由於自民黨已在國會選舉取得空前壓倒性勝利，產

業補貼計畫將可落實，讓政府投資不僅貢獻短期 GDP，亦可提升日本長期經濟競爭力。
日本 2 月剔除生鮮食品的消費者價格年比上漲 1.6%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最小升幅，主要因為
公用事業補貼壓低能源成本。日本勞動組合總連合會(連合)近期公布 2026 年春鬥第 1 次彙整結果，
加薪幅度雖低於去年同期 、 不過已連3 年高於 5%，且2026 年中小企業加薪幅度可望高於過去幾年，
支撐日本薪資物價形成正向循環，並達成實質薪資增長。近期伊朗戰事導致油價飆升，首相高市早苗
積極應對，除釋出戰備儲油外，將控制汽油價格上限，故預料短期通膨不致大幅躍昇。
日本央行三月利率決策會議維持政策不變，主要考量來自中東局勢不確定性，難以衡量伊朗戰爭
及國際油價飆漲對日本經濟及通膨帶來影響 ，呼應日本央行總裁植田和男論點 ， 希望有更多時間可以
觀察對物價的引導。後市央行升息時點須留意4 月初將公布的短觀報告，若企業信心尚維持正面，搭
配伊朗戰爭如白宮所預期的在 5、6 周之內結束，則日本央行或有望在第二季升息一碼。預料升息進

度放緩將使十年期日本公債殖利率在 2.0~2.5%區間震盪，且由於全球央行方向轉鷹、美元受惠於避
險需求，日本央行緩步升息有助日圓匯率穩定。
波克夏將對東京海上進行出資 ， 在4 月藉由收購東京海上持有的自家庫藏股 、 取得東京海上2.49%
股權，出資額為 2,874 億日圓。在巴菲特掌舵時期，波克夏對日本企業的投資以大型商社為主，此次
將是波克夏首度和日本金融機構進行合作 ， 性質與過去投資商社股不同 ， 若波克夏未來對日投資可擴
及其他非商社股，對於日股長期表現將帶來更大力度的加持。
2026 年以來日股受惠於高市擴大財政刺激 、強化軍事安全、鞏固美日同盟上，但本益比仍在 18.38
倍，股價淨值比在 1.68 倍。3 月下旬外資賣超日股，估計是受到伊朗戰事干擾且避免股利發放的課
稅而調整持股部位所致。近期日圓震盪走貶，有利於於日本外銷股財報表現，預料企業獲利可望為日
股在全球金融市場震盪下帶來支撐。

新興亞洲股市：中東戰事對經濟影響負面，但成長仍具韌性
亞洲股市 3 月因中東情勢緊張而大跌，截至 3/27，亞洲不含日本指數 (MXAPJ) 單月下跌
10.39%。南韓股市跌幅居前，單月下跌 12.90%，多次於盤中啟動熔斷機制，除了中東地緣政治風
險之外，漲幅過大後的技術性修正也是主因。台股在內資支撐下，跌幅相對較輕，單月下跌 6.50%。
中國股市也表現弱勢，上證指數下跌 5.99%，國企指數下跌 4.58%。3 月份正值全國兩會結束，股市
出現了「利多出盡」的調節。MSCI 東協指數 3 月下跌 3.53%，是跌幅較輕的亞洲區域。馬來西亞與

印尼身為原油、液化天然氣（LNG）及棕櫚油的重要出口國，油價上漲直接帶動其相關權值股走強，
抵銷了跌幅，但越南、菲律賓等原油進口國，跌幅超過 10%。印度 SENSEX 指數下跌 9.48%，印度
是全球第三大石油進口國，約 85% 的原油依賴進口，油價高漲對經濟造衝擊，另外外資於三月大量
賣出，也是印度股市重挫的原因。
亞洲經濟在 2026 年 Q2 將面對能源價格的劇烈波動對經濟的衝擊。根據 ADB 資料，泰國、
南韓與新加坡及印度等國，其能源進口依賴度顯著高於區域平均 。在當前中東衝突陰影未散的背景
下，油價的每一次跳動都觸發亞洲通膨的扳機，對於這些原油高依賴進口國家而言，能源成本的上升
不僅僅是物價問題，更是對 GDP 增長動能的直接侵蝕。
根據野村的推估「油價每上漲 10%」對各國經濟的衝擊路徑各不相同，印度與菲律賓的 CPI 通
膨壓力最為顯著，預計分別跳升 0.5 個百分點，這將迫使兩國央行在 Q2 維持較高利率環境，壓縮
降息空間 。反觀馬來西亞與印尼，受惠於其資源生產國的地位，GDP 增長甚至可能出現微幅的正向

回饋或較小的負面衝擊 。這種「能源非對稱性」將導致亞洲內部經濟與貨幣政策的進一步分化。
然而亞洲經濟在 2026 年 Q2 面臨增長放緩的挑戰。但根據博鰲亞洲論壇報告表示，受國際貿易不
確定性及地緣政治風險上升影響，今年亞洲增長率預計從去年的 4.7% 降至 4.5%。儘管增速微降，
但亞洲經濟體 GDP 占世界比重持續上升，依然是全球經濟的主要成長引擎。這與 ADB 資料相呼
應，顯示亞洲在全球經濟增速放緩的大環境下，憑藉區域一體化進程展現出較佳的韌性 。
對於印度與菲律賓等高通膨敏感區，應關注其內需韌性能否抵銷能源稅的負面影響；而對於新加
坡、南韓與台灣等雖然進口佔比較高但產業鏈地位穩固的經濟體，仍看好在全球 AI 供應鏈帶來的快
速增長。整體而言亞洲國家在 2026 年的經濟增長整體保持韌性，但將出現明確分化。

中國股市：轉型陣痛與外部地緣風險的夾擊
2026 年中國兩會不僅發佈經濟目標，更是「十五五規劃」首年的轉型宣言。官方將 GDP 成長
目標設定在 4.5% 至 5.0% 的區間，這項數據釋放了一個明確信號，北京已不再盲目追求過往的高
速增長，而是將重心置於「新質生產力」的質變與落地。在政策宣示中，我們觀察到由「總量擴張」
向 「結構優化」 的顯著移轉。2026 年數位經濟核心產業佔 GDP 比重目標提升至 12.5%，重點布局
人工智能（AI Agent）與具身智能（Physical AI）的工業化應用。同時，政府推動的大規模「設備更

新與消費品以舊換新」 政策，試圖在傳統房產動能消退之際，透過激發存量市場的替代需求來維持內
需熱度。這種以新經濟與高品質消費為主的政策組合，旨在對沖房地產下行對內需產生的結構性缺
口，並賦予國家經濟轉型的重任。
中國經濟正經歷一場歷史性的動能切換，長期作為增長引擎的房地產，其最終需求對 GDP 的佔
比正持續回落。與此同時，以醫藥、先進設備、IT 通訊及研發為首的「高科技 + 新三樣」產業，其
貢獻權重已經在 2025 年正式超越房地產。轉型過程雖然伴隨著陣痛，卻是中國邁向科技自主的必要
路徑 。 儘管房地產的長尾效應仍在消化 ， 但這些新興領域的快速擴張正為中國經濟建立更具韌性的基
礎，成為觀察中國能否維持區域增速預期的核心支柱。對於投資者而言，這意味著舊有的投資邏輯已
徹底改變，未來市場的超額收益將高度集中在具備全球競爭力的先進製造龍頭。
儘管新經濟轉型積極，但房地產市場的下滑壓力依然是中國最沉重的負擔。根據彭博模型，房地
產尋底之路極為漫長。預測路徑顯示，未來幾年房地產佔 GDP 比重將持續收縮至 12% 左右。中

國的政策博弈重點在於政府如何透過擴張性財政手段來進行 「止血」 ，防止房市超跌引發系統性金融
風險。但在目前市場信心修復極為緩慢的背景下，觀察銷售數據能否在超跌區間出現初步止穩。
然而中國面臨的外部環境更加嚴峻。中東危機的突然升溫，成為干擾復甦的關鍵變數。地緣震盪
推升了能源溢價，加劇輸入型通膨壓力，美國聯準會（Fed）態度的轉向鷹派與美元持續走強，顯著
壓縮了中國央行的降息空間。在強美元與高利差的雙重擠壓下，人民幣匯率面臨巨大的貶值壓力。這
種「內需待援、外部受限」的政策困局，使得操作空間顯得倍加受限，雖然兩會釋放了擴張性財政的
積極信號，但在地緣政治風險與強勢美元背景下，市場缺乏強勁的資金流入動能，也因此我們認為中
國股市欠缺全面性上漲的機會。類股建議在配置上規避傳統房地產產業，鎖定受惠於新質生產力政
策、具備技術護城河且能抵禦外部波動的科技領先標的。

印度股市： 中東戰事與 Fed 轉鷹影響印度成長動能
印度的實體經濟活動成長發出趨緩信號。3 月份最新發布的 HSBC Flash 印度綜合 PMI 指數由 2
月的 58.9 降至 56.5，創下 2022 年 10 月以來最弱的擴張速度，反映出國內需求在物價壓力下正
逐步降溫 。其中，製造業 PMI 降至 53.8，顯示國內訂單成長受阻；然而，值得關注的是國際訂單
卻創下調查歷史新高，這證明在全球供應鏈重組的戰略大環境下，印度製造的外部需求依然堅挺 。
儘管基本面仍具韌性，但 PMI 所揭示的投入成本增速創多年新高，預示著企業利潤空間將面臨嚴峻

的輸入型通膨擠壓，這將是觀察印度經濟能否維持高成長的首要指標。
儘管短期數據出現波動，但印度作為全球增長引擎的核心地位並未動搖。在 2026-27 財政預算
案的架構下， 政府持續推動以基礎設施投資為核心的擴張性政策 ， 試圖透過提升公共支出效率來強化
製造業基礎。印度正積極利用其人口紅利與政策激勵，吸引從電子組裝到半導體測試的全球業者進
駐。然而，這種高度依賴資本流入與外部能源供給的增長模式，在地緣政治動盪與聯準會轉向鷹派的
環境下，正迎來一場壓力測試。「內部政策利多」與「外部環境利空」的互相干擾，使得印度的成長
前景短期面臨不確定性 。
能源價格震盪是印度經濟短期最重要的潛在風險。根據彭博經濟的報導，印度央行估計，原油價
格每上漲 10 美元，經濟成長將會下降 0.15%。若中東衝突導致霍爾木茲海峽關閉三個月，2027 財
年成長率恐重挫至 6.1% 。在極端情況下，燃料成本飆升將引發連鎖反應，不僅削弱消費者的實際收
入，更會因國營石油公司虧損削減股利，進而擠壓預算案中的公共建設支出與政府投資能力。除了價

格波動，供應端的物理性中斷亦是威脅。印度的能源約 85%依賴進口，若航運中斷導致供應缺口，
包含民生消費與石化產業等經濟活動都將受到供應鍊斷裂的風險。
在貨幣政策上 ， 印度央行面臨 「內外夾擊」 。 若油價衝向140 美元 ，CPI 預計將進一步升 5.0%，
並可能在 9 月突破 RBI 的 6%容忍區間，央行被迫在年底臨時升息 2 碼。外部方面，聯準會態度的轉
向鷹派加劇了資金回流美元資產的壓力，也將干擾印度央行的寬鬆節奏。綜合上述分析，我們對印度
市場看法轉向中性。雖然結構性紅利與財政擴張確保了其增長基本盤 ， 但近期的重心已由 「追求擴張」
轉向「防禦通膨與能源衝擊」，這讓 PMI 顯示內需趨緩。加上潛在的油價的大漲與供應減少，正大
幅抵銷供應鏈轉移的長期利多。我們建議在股市配置上採取守勢。在近期的估值修正期，利用市場震
盪，重新評估基本面。

### Page 8

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
拉美股市：巴西正式啟動降息循環，中東衝突有利拉美經濟
巴西央行貨幣政策委員會(Copom)於 3 月 18 日會議全票通過將基準利率調降 25 個基點至
14.75%，正式啟動新一輪降息循環。Copom 會議紀要強調該決定與當前高度不確定性的背景相符，
在地緣政治衝突持續時間與範圍難以預判 、 經濟活動放緩步調及其對物價水準影響的訊號錯綜複雜之
下，保持從容並收集額外資訊為適當之舉。Copom 也為下次會議將降息步調加速至 50 個基點保
留空間，但也強調需美伊戰爭等外部環境改善且這些事件對通膨與通膨預期影響有限才能達成。

市場通膨預期方面，截至 3 月 30 日當周巴西央行市場調查顯示，2026 年通膨預期上升 14
個基點至 4.31%，高於巴西央行 3.0% 的長期通膨目標中點，2027、2028 及 2029 年則分別為
3.84%、3.57% 及 3.50%，惟有鑑於近期油價持續走升， 預計未來數週通膨預期將可能進一步惡化。
此外，市場對 2026 年底 Selic 利率中位預期維持在 12.50%，2027 及 2028 年底分別為 10.50%
及 10.00%，顯示雖中東戰爭帶動市場通膨升溫，然而巴西市場整體降息格局並未受到太大影響，仍
是朝大幅降息的方向前進。
另一方面，雖美伊戰爭帶動短線油價上漲，帶動拉美市場通膨預期，然而油價上漲事實上也有利
於拉美整體經濟，主因拉美市場中，包含阿根廷、巴西、哥倫比亞與厄瓜多等國為石油淨出口國，高
盛研究指出若油價上漲 10%，能使整體拉美市場未來 12 個月 GDP 上升約 0.2%，顯示美伊戰爭
對拉美市場來說並非全然只有負面影響。
最後一個值得關注的議題是巴西 2026 年的總統大選，市場主要關注現任總統盧拉，與前總統

波索納洛之子弗拉維奧的對決。市調機構 Quaest 3 月民調結果顯示，在第二輪決選中，兩位候選人
首度打成平手，各獲得 41% 支持度，扭轉過往皆由盧拉勝出的局勢，顯示中東衝突引發的能源價格
上漲與通膨壓力持續影響盧拉政權的支持度 。市場預期若最終總統大選由右派陣營的弗拉維奧勝出，
其政策將會有利於巴西財政赤字問題改善，相較於盧拉政營財政擴張的策略 ，更可能吸引外資流入巴
西市場。
展望後市 ，儘管中東衝突帶動拉美市場通膨預期攀升 ，然而拉美市場最大經濟體巴西仍處於降息
循環中，且目前市場並不預期巴西央行會因為能源價格問題，而扭轉寬鬆方向，此外，拉美市場整體
亦會受惠於油價上漲帶動的石油出口助益，故整體而言，我們目前對拉美市場後市仍抱持正向看法。

能源類股：美伊停戰協商短期難有結論，可以資產配置角度布局能源類股
美伊衝突仍是 3 月主導能源價格走勢的關鍵，雖然美國在 3 月 25 日透過巴基斯坦向伊朗提出停
戰協議，然而內容與伊朗訴求相差甚遠，預料短期內美伊達成停戰協議的可能性並不高，戰事仍可能
需要來回數次的武力攻擊與談判後，才能底定。
隨戰爭持續，荷莫茲海峽的中斷程度仍是油價最關鍵的變數，戰前荷莫茲海峽每天約有 130 艘
船通過海峽 ， 目前每天僅有不到 6 艘在伊朗許可下通行，海峽運輸仍幾乎處於癱瘓狀態 。 對伊朗而言 ，
封鎖荷莫茲海峽越久 ， 越能為其換得更好的談判籌碼 ， 伊朗國會目前更是在推動立法對通過海峽的船

舶收取過路費，以此將海峽視為經濟收入來源，故何時荷莫茲海峽能夠暢通，仍具高度不確定性。
受荷莫茲海峽運量持續受阻影響，高盛 3 月 23 日報告再度上修油價預測，預估 3 月布蘭特原油
平均價格將達 105 美元，4 月將進一步上升至 115 美元，並在 2026 年第四季回落至 80 美元，該預
測基於荷莫茲海峽石油流量將在 6 週內維持極低水準的假設。此外，在海峽流量中斷 10 週的不利情
境下，高盛預估布蘭特油價將可能接近或超越 2008 年 147 美元的歷史紀錄，隨後在2026 年第四季
放緩至 100 美元；在中斷10 週並同時伴隨基礎設施受損的嚴重不利情境下，油價可能飆至接近160
美元，並在2026 年第四季仍維持 115 美元高油價。在高盛各種情境分析中，若海峽流量中斷時間延
長，或者有更多能源基礎設施受損，長期油價都存在上升的可能性，有利能源類股表現。
另一方面，觀察 Bloomberg 西德州原油期貨遠期曲線，可以看出目前市場仍定價荷莫茲海峽受
阻為中短期事件，曲線在開戰後前端快速上揚，反映現貨端供給緊張狀況，然而遠端合約變動幅度不

大，且近幾個月合約曲線陡峭，顯示市場認為荷莫茲海峽受阻仍將是短期事件，並不預期戰爭將會演
變成長期戰事，預料隨衝突結束，原油供給將大幅恢復，帶動油價下跌。3 月 30 日 WTI 遠期曲線顯
示，油價將會在 11 月回落至每桶 80 美元以下。此外，截至 3/30，國際賭盤 Polymarket 預估美伊
在 4/15 前停戰機率約 17%，4/30 前為 32%，6/30 前為 59%，12/31 前則為 75%， 機率在 4/30
至 6/30 區間增幅最大，隱含市場對停火的預期主要落在 4~6 月。
展望後市，雖然近期美伊皆提出談判條件，然而雙方在談判條件上仍存在顯著落差，再加上對於
伊朗來說，將荷莫茲海峽封鎖的越久，讓能源價格維持高檔，越能為伊朗得到更好的談判籌碼，故預
估伊朗更可能採取邊打邊談的策略，將整體談判時程拉長。對於能源類股來說，短線上若戰爭持續，
能源類股有望走出獨立行情，並可作為投資組合中對衝戰爭風險的角色，而即使在停戰後，長期油價
仍有望高於戰前水準，亦有利支撐能源類股表現，故建議可以資產配置角度，適度布局能源類股。

黃金類股：美伊戰事壓抑短線金價表現，惟反轉條件已逐步成形
黃金傳統上被視為戰爭期間的避險資產，然而本波自 2 月 28 日美伊戰爭爆發以來，金價卻明顯
表現不佳，從戰前約 5,100 美元一路下跌，一度回撤至約 4,100 美元位置。本波金價走疲，主因美伊
戰爭導致荷莫茲海峽受阻，持續推升油價，帶動市場通膨預期上升，同步推高了美國公債殖利率，使
不孳息的黃金資產吸引力下降。此外，市場避險情緒亦帶動美元走強，而黃金價格長期以來與美元走
勢呈負相關，隨美元指數走升，黃金等以美元計價之商品價格亦出現承壓。
此外，黃金重要買盤全球央行，近期購金動能也出現明顯放緩。世界黃金協會報告指出，2026

年 1 月全球央行淨購金量驟降至 5 噸，較去年月均購金量 27 噸銳減近 80%，推測主因 1 月金價波
動過大，導致各國央行態度轉為觀望。 另一方面，土耳其央行近期大幅減持黃金 ，也對市場情緒造
成壓抑。據彭博新聞引述知情人士報導，土耳其央行在 3 月 13 日至 3 月 20 日的兩周內，以出售及
協議交換置換了約 60 噸黃金，用以穩定因美伊戰爭而受影響的土耳其里拉匯率，彭博指出，土耳其
央行本次減持黃金，一部分是採市場直接拋售，一部分則是通過 SWAP 以黃金作為抵押品來獲取外
匯或里拉 ，而歷史上各國央行透過 SWAP 以貴金屬獲得廉價美元融資的情況並不少見，且回顧 2023
年 3 月至 5 月期間，土耳其央行亦曾售出近 160 噸黃金，惟當時因來自黃金 ETF 的買家吸收賣壓，
對短線金價並未造成太大影響，故若僅有來自土耳其央行的一次性賣壓，實際並不需要太過擔憂。
更需要留意的是 ， 中東戰爭將可能迫使其他國家同樣採取出售黃金儲備的動作，以支撐外匯存底
或籌措資金，若此趨勢擴大，將 可能進一步增加黃金短線賣壓， 同時，因目前市場資金較不偏好黃

金，短線上黃金 ETF 吸納來自各國央行賣壓的力道也會較弱，故只要美伊戰爭持續，黃金類股短線
表現將可能受到較大壓力。
即便如此，我們仍可看出世界央行對中長期增持黃金儲備抱持正向態度 ，例如在 1 月份的世界央
行黃金買家中，就意外的出現了馬來西亞央行，為該國自 2018 年以來首度增持黃金儲備，另外在 3
月初，韓國央行也宣布計畫從 2026 年第一季起，將黃金 ETF 納入其外匯準備投資組合，顯示各國
央行對於增持黃金儲備的長期方向仍未改變 。世界黃金協會也預估 ，預期全球央行在 2026 年將會增
持約 850 噸黃金，增持水準將會與去年相近，顯示未來世界央行購金買盤仍可望為金價帶來支撐。
此外，展望後市 ，金價反轉的條件實際上也已在逐步成形，當前市場直覺認為高油價將導致通膨 ，
預期聯準會動作會偏鷹，導致金價表現受到壓抑。然而若戰事在短期內結束，預料市場將忽略短期
油價上漲的影響，使降息預期大致回升到戰前水準，有利金價表現；而若美伊戰爭 進一步擴大，將
可能使油價被推升至壓抑經濟表現的價格，屆時經濟更可能面臨的問題反而不是通膨而是衰退， 聯

準會更可能降息以救經濟，隨利率走低亦有利金價反彈。故 目前來看，隨金價回檔已深，重回多頭
條件也已在逐步成形，建議短線可持續觀望戰事變化，中性看待黃金類股後市表現。

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：通膨疑慮雖推升利率，未來仍有降息可能
FOMC 3/18 會議釋出訊號略偏鷹派。投票結果 11 比 1，僅 Miran 主張降息一碼，而原先偏鴿
的 Waller 轉為支持按兵不動，內部對降息的共識正在弱化。會後聲明雖新增中東局勢不確定性的描
述，釋出政策需保持耐心的訊號，但整體對經濟評估仍維持「穩健」 ，未因2 月就業數據轉弱而顯著
調整措辭。點陣圖中位數雖未變動，但分布結構出現轉變。首先，預期降息的官員人數減少，支持多
次降息的比例下滑；其次，已出現個別官員預期未來升息的情境；此外，長期利率中位數上修，顯示

中性利率評估提高。
雖然因中東能源問題導致 Fed Funds 隔夜利率交換市場所估算今年底前的利率變動預期已從戰
事爆發前的降息 2 碼大幅縮減，近期更出現不排除升息的臆測。然而，自美以伊衝突以來至 3/26(台
北時間)， 包括高盛在內的投資銀行所發布對Fed 利率(區間上緣)預期均認為 FOMC 未來兩年仍將傾
向降息。若此預期得以實現，代表市場目前已過度反映 Fed 的「鷹派情境」，之後殖利率可望有回
穩的空間。
再者，3 月油價上揚，投資人對於未來美國貨幣政策轉向的疑慮升溫，確實也導致了美國甚至是
全球的主要債券市場殖利率上揚。然而，就在市場聚焦於通膨的同時，包括 Pimco、摩根大通和
Columbia Threadneedle Investments 等投資機構 3/30 不約而同表達，投資人低估了美國對伊朗
戰爭導致經濟急劇放緩的風險的看法，並認為這場衝擊最終將引發債券殖利率再度回落 。高盛 3 月下
旬的報告提醒，比起升息，中東戰事更大的風險在於(接近)經濟衰退，這可能觸發 Fed 更大幅度的降

息。Pimco 首席投資官 Daniel Ivascyn 也表示「通常始於通膨衝擊的局面，會迅速演變為成長衝擊…
而我們正處於經濟大幅走軟的邊緣。」因此，未來債券殖利率不排除有向下的可能性存在。
隨著美國 11 月期中選舉逼近，川普在政治上將面臨更大的壓力，以美國財政體質及川普向來的
政策來看，之後美國政府勢必將盡力壓低美國公債的殖利率，有利使美債利率處於相對可控的範圍。
近期美國公債殖利率雖因市場的通膨疑慮而上揚 ， 但目前殖利率遠高於Fed 長期通膨目標(2%)，
美債的高息收對長線投資人具強大誘因 ， 因此投資人仍可以資產配置角度對於債券作適當之配置 。 此
外，包括 Pimco、摩根大通等投資機構也認為未來債券殖利率有向下的可能性存在。以目前的殖利
率看，美債仍有投資空間。考量短期殖利率受市場對 Fed 的利率政策預期變動影響較大，故在年期
與券種的選擇上可以 7~15 年的中長期美國公債或投資級債為主。

Auras Technology (3324.TWO)
05 August 2026 Citi Research
3
Auras Technology
Valuation
We value Auras using a PE-based approach, reflecting its accelerating earnings trajectory and rising share in liquid cooling
market of AI servers. Our TP of NT$1,480 is based on 23x average 2026E-27E EPS, which we believe is justified give the
company’s expanding server revenue base, deeper engagement with CSP customers, and broader product portfolio across cold

plates, CDMs, and CDUs.
Risks
This stock is High Risk based upon our quantitative model, but assigning a High Risk rating is not supported in our view by
other qualitative factors such as rapid RVL progress and Thailand expansion, and so a High Risk rating has not been applied.
Key downside risks to our investment thesis and target price include: 1) slower adoption of liquid cooling; 2) heightened price
competition; 3) lower-than-expected share in liquid cooling components; and 4) emerging alternative cooling technologies.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

## Document 194: 3个月狂砸600亿美元！Anthropic再签91亿算力大单，与比特币矿企Riot达成长约

Source PDF: all report pdf/3个月狂砸600亿美元！Anthropic再签91亿算力大单，与比特币矿企Riot达成长约.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-08-11
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 斥资 91 亿美元与⽐特币矿企 Riot Platforms 签署 20 年算⼒⻓约，供应 191 兆⽡算
⼒，若⾏使延期选项总价值最⾼达 161 亿美元。这是 Anthropic 近⽉连签的第三笔巨额算⼒
协议，三笔合计规模已超 600 亿美元。消息公布后， Riot 股价盘后暴涨 25% ，其 " 挖矿转
AI" 的估值逻辑获市场强烈认可。
摘要：
AI 独⻆兽 Anthropic 正以前所未有的速度扩张算⼒版图。
据彭博社周⼆报道，知情⼈⼠透露，Anthropic 已与⽐特币矿企 Riot Platforms 达成⼀项价值 91
亿美元的云计算⻓期合约，协议期限⻓达 20 年。这是 Anthropic 近期连续签署的多笔⼤规模算⼒
采购协议之⼀，折射出 AI 企业在算⼒供给端⾯临的持续压⼒。消息公布后， Riot 股价盘后⼤涨
25% ⾄ 24.40 美元。
此次合约涵盖 191 兆⽡的算⼒供应，相当于同⼀时刻为约 14.3 万⼾家庭提供电⼒，算⼒来源为
Riot 位于德克萨斯州 Rockdale 的数据中⼼园区。若双⽅⾏使合同中两次各五年的延期选项，合

同总价值最⾼可达 161 亿美元，协议将延续⾄ 2048 年 6 ⽉。
Anthropic 密集布局算⼒，近⽉连签巨额⻓约
算⼒短缺已成为 Anthropic 业务扩张的核⼼瓶颈。Anthropic 近期已相继与多家算⼒供应商签署
⼤额协议，以应对旗下 Claude 系列 AI ⼯具持续攀升的客⼾需求。
就在本次 Riot 协议之前， Anthropic 刚刚与成⽴仅数⽉的基础设施初创公司 Volta Infra Holdings 签
署了⼀笔 100 亿美元的云计算协议；今年 5 ⽉， Anthropic 还与⻢斯克旗下 xAI 达成协议，承诺采
购近 450 亿美元的算⼒资源。三笔协议合计规模已超过 600 亿美元，显⽰出这家 Claude 开发商在
算⼒采购上的激进姿态。
Riot 转型 AI 数据中⼼，押注算⼒需求红利
Riot Platforms 的此次合作，是其从加密货币挖矿向 AI 云计算转型战略的重要落地。这家公司曾
以 Bioptix 为名，最初从事⽣物科技⾏业诊断设备制造，后⼤幅转型专注⽐特币挖矿，如今再度

调整⽅向，开始向 AI 数据中⼼领域拓展。
Riot 此前已宣布与 Advanced Micro Devices 达成单独合作协议，共同建设算⼒基础设施。 Riot 在
周⼀早些时候披露的 2026 年第⼆季度财报显⽰，其季度总营收超出市场预期，数据中⼼业务是
重要贡献来源之⼀。
Riot 并⾮孤例。多家加密货币企业正借助其在电⼒基础设施和⼤规模数据中⼼运营⽅⾯的既有积
累，加速切⼊ AI 算⼒市场，以期从本轮 AI 基础设施建设热潮中分得⼀杯羹。
⻓约结构锁定收益， Riot 估值逻辑重塑
从合约结构来看，此次协议对 Riot 的财务前景具有实质性意义。 91 亿美元的基础合同收益，叠加
两次延期选项带来的潜在增量，使 Riot 的⻓期营收能⻅度⼤幅提升，也为市场重新评估其估值提
供了依据。

## Document 259: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 미국 생산거점과 5공장, 그리고 펩타이드삼성바이오로직스(207940) 미국 생산거점과 5공장, 그리고 펩타이드미국 생산거점과 5공장, 그리고 펩타이드 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 미국 생산거점과 5공장, 그리고 펩타이드삼성바이오로직스(207940) 미국 생산거점과 5공장, 그리고 펩타이드미국 생산거점과 5공장, 그리고 펩타이드 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-07-24
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
2분기 실적 예상치 부합 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 2분기 연결 영업실적은 매출액 1조 3,209억 원(+30.2% 
Y oY), 영업이익 5,864억 원(+22.9% Y oY)으로 시장 예상치에 대체로 부합했다.  
1~4공장의 풀가동과 우호적인 원/달러 환율이 외형 성장을 견인했으며, 5공장 
인증용 배치 생산과 미국 록빌 공장 운영에 따른 초기 비용이 선반영됐음에도 
영업이익률 44.4%의 견조한 수익성을 유지했다.  
2026년 연간 연결 매출액은 5조 4,729억 원(+20.1% Y oY), 영업이익은 2조 4,548억 
원(+18.6% Y oY)으로 추정한다. 2분기 약 1,500억 원 규모의 생산 차질이 
발생했으나, 일부 물량이 연내 생산될 예정이며 3분기부터 록빌 공장의 매출 기여가 
본격화되고 5공장 생산도 확대될 것으로 예상한다. 이에 따라 생산 차질이 연간 
실적에 미치는 영향은 제한적이며, 연간 매출 성장률은 가이던스 15~20%의 상단을 
달성할 것으로 전망한다.  
비만 치료제 성장에 올라타는 CDMO 
삼성바이오로직스는 스위스 폴리펩타이드그룹 인수를 통해 항체 중심의 CDMO 
사업을 펩타이드 분야로 확장할 계획이다. 폴리펩타이드의 2025년 매출은 약 
6,400억 원, EBITDA는 770억 원을 기록했으며, 2028년에는 매출액 약 1조 원, 
EBITDA 약 2,600억 원으로 성장할 전망이다. 올해 말까지 지분 100%를 확보해 
연결 자회사로 편입할 경우 동사의 외형 성장에 크게 기여할 것으로 예상한다. 특히 
비만·당뇨 치료제 시장의 고성장으로 펩타이드 원료의약품 수요가 빠르게 확대되는 
가운데, 이번 인수를 통해 신규 성장 시장에 진입하고 사업 포트폴리오를 다각화할 
수 있을 것으로 기대한다.

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

## Document 264: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 90 – 20 = 70LG에너지솔루션(373220) 90 – 20 = 7090 – 20 = 70 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 90 – 20 = 70LG에너지솔루션(373220) 90 – 20 = 7090 – 20 = 70 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 2분기, ESS가 예상 대비 부진 
LG에너지솔루션의 2Q26 매출액 7조 5,600억원(QoQ +15.3%), 영업이익 1,130억원
(QoQ 흑전, AMPC 2,410억원 반영), OPM 1.5%(QoQ +4.7%p)로 영업이익 기준 컨
센서스(매출액 7.2조원, 영업이익 1,880억원)를 하회했다. 컨센서스 대비 영업이익이 
750억원 하회한 배경으로는 ESS 매출이 가이던스인 +40% 보다 하회한 +31.6%로 
부진한 영향이다. ESS 생산량이 예상 대비 부진한 이유는 셀 생산은 원활했으나 팩, 
컨테이너 CAPA가 병목으로 작용한 영향이다. ESS는 부진했으나 EV는 유럽을 중심
으로 VW, 르노 등 중저가 전기차 판매가 확대되었으며, T사와 R사향의 소형전지 판
매 호조로 매출액은 컨센서스에 부합했다. 
26년 3분기, ESS QoQ +42%로 ESS가 견인할 분기 
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26 매출액 9조 470억원(QoQ +19.7%), 영업이익 2,930억원
(QoQ 160,0% AMPC 3,860억원 반영), OPM 3.2%(QoQ +1.7%p)로 전망한다. ESS 
연간 신규 수주 목표는 90GWh로 상반기 ESS 신규 수주가 약 20GWh 수준임을 고
려시 하반기 70GWh의 신규 수주가 기대된다. 2분기부터 랜싱과 UC2가 양산이 시
작되며 동사의 글로벌 ESS CAPA 는 68GWh(중국 7GWh, 한국 1GWh, 미국 
57GWh, 폴란드 3GWh)이며 미국 CAPA 57GWh는 홀란드 17GWh, STL 10GWh, 
Honda 10GWh, 랜싱 10GWh, UC2 10GWh로 구분된다. 
투자의견 매수, 목표주가 560,000원 유지한다. 
LG에너지솔루션에 대한 투자의견은 매수, 목표주가는 560,000원으로 유지한다. 
2026년 가이던스는 매출액 Y oY +15~20% 수준, OPM은 +MS% 제시했으며 연 내 
충분히 달성 가능할 것으로 전망한다. 얼티엄 공장의 가동은 8월로 예정되어 있어  
EV 부문의 AMPC 수취가 3분기부터 본격적으로 시작될 것으로 예상한다. 
목표주가 (유지) 560,000원

## Document 301: Capital Market Outlook (2026-07-27)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-07-27).pdf
Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)
Date: 2026-07-27
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 July 27, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Moderate Growth, Anchored Inflation, Resilient Risk Appetite
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Incoming data suggest U.S. growth remains moderate and close to potential rather than
overheating. Activity is being pulled in both directions: wealth effects, inflation-indexed
government transfers, government spending, tax incentives and the AI investment boom

continue to support it—while higher oil prices and tariffs, slower wage growth, cautious
bank lending, elevated consumer borrowing costs and soft hiring restrain it.
The Federal Reserve’s (Fed) Beige Book prepared for the July 28–29 rate-setting meeting,
supports this view. Over the mid-May to early July reference period, 11 of 12 Fed districts
reported expansion, the broadest improvement since early 2025. Yet, the expansion was
characterized as slight to moderate, with moderate price increases, wage growth modest

to moderate, and employment rising on balance but showing little to no change in seven
of 12 districts. Recent ADP data also show that four-week average employment gains
have started to fade through the July 4 week after picking up earlier this year.
With slower and narrower job creation (private-sector employment outside healthcare
and education is still lower than two years ago), labor-market slack has grown. The
average duration of unemployment has increased, job openings have declined and the

pool of people outside the labor force who nevertheless want a job has expanded. The
lack of labor-market dynamism—partly due to a widening mismatch between jobs and
skills as the economy changes and technology advances—has also restrained wage
growth, constraining consumer spending.
Indeed, although June retail sales surprised to the upside, the underlying signal was less
upbeat than the headline suggested. According to BofA Global Research, non-store retailers

accounted for much of the strength, helped by special promotions that could partly
reverse in July. Spending in most other categories remained tepid, with limited evidence
of a meaningful net boost from the World Cup. While upward revisions to prior months,
especially to food services, lifted expectations for Q2 real consumer spending after a very
weak Q1 gain, real gross domestic product (GDP) still tracks around 1.75% to 2% for Q2,
according to various estimates, consistent with moderate, non-inflationary growth outside

energy-price effects. In fact, renewed fuel-price pressures, alongside fading support from
early-year tax refunds, point to persistent growth headwinds in the second half.
Manufacturing tells a similarly mixed story. After two strong monthly gains earlier in
the year, output increased modestly in May and contracted in June (Exhibit 1A). The AI
investment boom is leaking into large imports of semiconductors and related equipment,
limiting its domestic growth and employment impact relative to headline investment

outlays. Also, a strong dollar restrains exports. On the other hand, lean inventories across
several industries point to a likely restocking cycle in coming quarters. In addition,
defense spending and rebounding aerospace production, alongside favorable regional
Fed July surveys point to continued manufacturing-sector expansion. This is consistent
with further improvement in the Institute for Supply Management manufacturing index—
typically a constructive signal for corporate earnings and investor risk appetite.

Against a backdrop of moderate growth, sharply lower June oil prices and fading tariff
effects, inflation delivered an encouraging downside surprise for a change. Not only did
June headline consumer price index (CPI) inflation decline sharply alongside lower oil prices,
but “core” CPI (excluding food and energy) also posted a small but rare monthly decline,
nudging the year-over-year (YoY) rate down to 2.6% from 2.8% in May. The improvement
was broad based: prices of goods excluding food and energy declined, prices for services

excluding energy and rent of shelter declined, and a larger share of CPI components (about
a third) declined. With housing still likely to contribute to future disinflation—as new-lease
measures remain soft and multifamily supply continues to expand—and little evidence
that tariffs or energy prices are feeding a broader wage-price spiral so far, gradual
inflation normalization without much Fed tightening is likely, in our view.
Investment Implications

With moderate U.S. growth and inflation
underpinnings, we expect investors to
continue to favor economically sensitive
sectors and AI-investment beneficiaries,
assuming stabilization of geopolitical
situation.

### Page 3

3 of 8 July 27, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
The main risk to the outlook remains energy. Although the global economy has adapted
better than expected to Strait of Hormuz disruptions, as discussed in recent reports,
renewed and expanded trade barriers raise risks to both growth and inflation. Ukrainian
attacks on Russian refining and export infrastructure have further tightened the global
refined-products market, with at least 24 of Russia’s 34 large refineries reportedly struck

by drones and bringing Russian crude processing down to its lowest level in more than
two decades. Given its large export footprint, especially for diesel, Russian diesel and jet
fuel export restrictions combined with gasoline imports have tightened refined-product
markets. India’s recent introduction of higher fuel export taxes and China’s preference for
preserving crude inventories while retaining large underutilized refining capacity reduce
the likelihood of near-term supply relief. In fact, the threatened blockade of the Bab-el-

Mandeb strait would extend the transit time for roughly three million barrels per day of
Saudi oil going to Asia by about a month, with additional logistical complexity tied to
Suez Canal tanker capacity constraints also increasing costs and transit time.
According to the EIA, with U.S. refiners running at full capacity, commercial crude
inventories remain above pre-2015 levels. However, refiners have prioritized jet fuel and
distillate production to meet strong global demand, causing gasoline inventories to fall

below their five-year range. Reflecting tight supply, U.S. gasoline and diesel prices have
risen to levels consistent with roughly $140 and $170 per barrel crude oil, respectively.
Moreover, fuel markets remain vulnerable to potential unexpected refinery maintenance
requirements. The Strategic Petroleum Reserve (SPR) has declined to 40-year lows and
is approaching operational limits.
Still, because of a big decline in the U.S. economy’s energy intensity, diesel and gasoline

costs as a share of GDP remain below past oil-shock levels (around 2.2% vs. roughly
4.5% in 1973, for example). Also, the U.S. is now an oil exporter rather than a large oil
importer. This suggests that oil prices are not high enough to cause a U.S. or global
recession. According to Dallas Fed Research, June 23, 2026, the estimated effect on U.S.
GDP growth in 1980 from a similar oil shock as that caused by the Hormuz crisis this
year would have had a 20 times larger impact (Exhibit 1B). Global fuel intensity has also

declined significantly, but the U.S. resilience is still greater. At current prices, there are
still sufficient growth tailwinds to keep the expansion going at a moderate pace. Also
positive, tempered growth and still anchored inflation expectations have helped keep the
10-year Treasury yield in its relatively narrow range of the past two years despite spiking
oil prices, with markets relatively comfortable with the gradual uptick in yields to date.

The bigger risk to asset prices would be a disorderly rise in real yields and bond-market
volatility, including if driven by Fed interest rate hikes without stronger growth.
Exhibit 1: Inflation Divergence Likely to Resolve to the Downside.
A) Industrial Production still moderate compared to past economic booms. B) Tremendously greater resilience to oil shock today compared to the past.

### Page 4

4 of 8 July 27, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
International Equity Markets—Strengths, Weaknesses,
Opportunities and Challenges
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
The drawdown in the global Technology sector between early-June and mid-July has
highlighted a major dividing line across international equity markets. Europe has been
the global leader over the period, emerging Asian markets have been by far the weakest,

and the U.S. and Japan have fallen in between the two. This has been a mirror image of
the full year 2026 so far, in which technology leadership has driven outperformance in
emerging markets and seen Europe lag. And it underscores the current importance of
the Technology sector to relative international returns, given the wide disparities now
present in regional capitalization weightings. Technology exposure (including the internet
platforms listed in Communication Services and Consumer Discretionary) now accounts

for 50% of the MSCI Emerging Market (EM) Index benchmark (and 60% of emerging
Asia), with the semiconductor industry group alone forming 35% of EM Equities (and
42% of emerging Asia).
By contrast, Europe’s Technology sector exposure stands at just 10%. Indeed, since the
start of 2025, the MSCI EM Index has outpaced the overall developed ex-U.S. benchmark
(MSCI World ex-U.S.) in 70% of the weekly periods in which the global Technology sector
has risen by more than global Equities (MSCI All-Country World) in aggregate (Exhibit 2).

Exhibit 2: Emerging Markets have Led Global Equities During Recent Periods of
Strength in the Technology Sector.

Sources: MSCI; Bloomberg. Data as of July 17, 2026. It is not possible to invest directly in an index. Please refer to index
definitions at the end of this report. Past performance is no guarantee of future results.
A key question for international markets in the second half of the year will therefore
be whether the advance in technology-linked markets can continue, particularly within
upstream segments related to AI data center investment. And important to watch

here will be the capital spending plans of the cloud hyperscalers, pricing power in
critical parts of the supply chain such as memory, foundry and semicap equipment, and,
ultimately, user demand for models and developer applications. Wider AI adoption and
more application use cases would likely allow market participation to extend further
downstream within the sector, including in China, whose capitalization share of the MSCI
EM index has halved over the past five years, but at close to 20% remains the third-

largest after Taiwan and Korea. And notwithstanding the potential for further bouts of
volatility of the type experienced over recent weeks, broader gains for the tech sector
should continue to favor EMs and Asia over Europe.
The return of energy supply constraints in the Middle East nonetheless poses a growing risk
to both European and Asian markets. Both are heavily reliant on imported oil and gas in their
primary energy consumption, with Asia more directly dependent on shipments from the

Middle East. And though both markets were able to withstand the disruptions to production,
shipping and prices during the first phase of the Iran conflict, the July re-escalation could
potentially be more challenging should it persist over the weeks and months ahead.
Investment Implications
We maintain a favorable view on EMs
relative to Europe, but continue to
weigh the potential impact of changes
to the macro environment related to
the pivotal themes of technology and

energy. We also stay cautious on the
UK market, particularly in light of this
month’s change in central government
leadership. And we remain constructive
on Japan, despite the market facing
similar risks to much of emerging Asia
from any further tightening in global
energy supply..

### Page 5

5 of 8 July 27, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
The MSCI Europe and MSCI emerging Asia benchmarks corrected by 10% to 15% in
March following the initial outbreak of the conflict but rebounded in Q2 even in the face
of higher energy prices as activity in the sector adjusted. Individual countries were able
to draw upon their national oil inventories. China in particular (by far the largest global
energy importer) reduced its oil imports by around 40%, taking them back to 2016 levels

when its economy was roughly half its current size (Exhibit 3). And oil and gas export
volumes were redirected to Saudi Arabia’s Red Sea coast.
Exhibit 3: China’s Oil Import Volumes have Fallen Sharply Since the Start of the
Iran Conflict.

But these three sources of relief are unlikely to offer the same cushion for markets in the
current phase of the conflict. Global observed oil inventories have now been drawn down
by more than 300 million barrels over the period between March and June, according

to the International Energy Agency. Depending on its willingness to further deplete
its domestic stockpiles, China’s oil import demand could begin to increase again from
depressed levels. And risks to energy exports through the Red Sea could force a diversion
of the estimated 5.4 million barrels per day of oil and 2.9 billion cubic feet per day of
liquefied natural gas that were transported through the waterway in Q1 of 2026, according
to the U.S. Energy Information Administration. It also remains to be seen how global

monetary authorities will react to higher headline inflation driven by the recent energy
price surge. Higher interest rates could present an additional hurdle for international equity
markets if central banks are forced to tighten policy. We maintain a favorable view on EMs
relative to Europe, but continue to weigh the potential impact of changes to the macro
environment related to these two pivotal themes of technology and energy.
Our view on the smaller regional developed markets also remains intact and has been

reinforced by recent local developments. As in continental Europe, we stay cautious on the
U.K. market, particularly in light of this month’s change in central government leadership.
Even though the ruling party remains in place and its platform is officially unchanged, the
new prime minister is expected to pursue a more redistributionist policy agenda and has
suggested introducing more flexibility into national budget rules with the potential for

future increases in tax rates. The fiscal outlook will therefore be important to watch over
the coming months for any potential deterioration in investor perceptions around the local
market. And, at the same time, we remain constructive on Japan. Given its dependence
on imported oil from the Middle East, the Japan market faces similar risks to much of
emerging Asia from any further tightening in global energy supply from the region. And
to a lesser extent, it should also remain sensitive to volatility in the Technology sector,

given its hardware exposure to industry groups such as semicap equipment and electronic
components. We nonetheless still see structural supports from shareholder-friendly
deployment of corporate cash for buybacks, payouts and acquisitions, in addition to fiscal
expansion coming from the new administration, and both competitive support and a
translation boost on overseas profits from yen weakness.

### Page 6

6 of 8 July 27, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Behind the Surge in U.S. Business Formation: The “Solopreneur”
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Business formation is at all-time highs in the U.S., with applications over the 12 months
through June 2026 running north of 6 million. 1 In other words, entrepreneurial activity
is booming in the U.S. But an underappreciated feature of the recent surge in business

applications has been the rise of the “solopreneur”—or solo founders who run businesses
on their own without employees.
As Exhibit 4A highlights, the increase in business applications since the start of 2025
has been primarily driven by solopreneurs. Whereas their applications have risen by 23%
in just the last year and a half, applications among “high-propensity” businesses (i.e.,
businesses likely to hire workers) are essentially flat over the same period. Translated:

Solo founders have fueled most of the recent acceleration in business creation. Data
from Stripe, a financial services platform, confirms in Exhibit 4B: The wave of new
startups, particularly in Q1 of this year, has largely been the work of solo founders.
What’s driving entrepreneurs to go solo? Enter generative artificial intelligence (AI), which
has lowered barriers to entry and enabled businesses to innovate, produce, market and
expand, all without necessarily hiring additional employees. Indeed, the sectors that have

seen the largest growth in business applications such as Information and Professional
Services are also the sectors with the highest AI adoption rates of 44% and 38%
respectively.
1 All the while, job growth in these sectors has been limited or negative.
Employment in the Information sector, for example, is currently below pre-pandemic
levels—despite the sector’s gross domestic product expanding by over 50% since the
start of the decade.
For investors, the rise of the solopreneur and the attendant increase in business

formation is a direct reflection of the U.S. economy’s entrepreneurial DNA. We believe
that's a key reason to maintain the U.S. at the center of portfolios because it means
more competition, greater innovation and, over time, higher productivity—all of which are
good news for corporate earnings and U.S. growth. Already, AI has helped lift company
margins with earnings as a share of GDP at a record 14% as of Q1 2026.
2 As AI adoption
grows and as the cost of technology declines, we expect new small businesses, the

backbone of the U.S. economy, to be among the biggest recipients of productivity gains.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

MSCI All-Country World Index is a premier benchmark representing global equity performance, covering large and mid-cap stocks across 23 developed and 24 emerging markets.
Institute for Supply Management Manufacturing Index is a composite measure equally weighing new orders, production, employment, supplier deliveries, and inventories.
MSCI Emerging Market Index is a stock market index designed to measure the equity market performance of developing economies globally.

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 310: Cliff Notes_ markets on edge (2026-08-12)

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: markets on edge
By Elliot Clarke, Ryan Wells&Illiana Jain
09:56 July 24 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
The June Labour Force Survey offered both noise and signal. Employment surprised materially to the
upside, rising 76.3k in the month. However, the gain was matched by a sizeable lift in labour supply, the
participation rate bouncing 0.3ppts to 67.0%, a 13-month high.

The ABS noted in the June release that there was a large group of individuals outside the labour force

### Page 2

with a job lined up in May, and that they began work in June. With the employment gain driven by new
labour force entrants, the unemployment rate held steady at 4.4% (edging up from 4.37% to 4.43% at
the second decimal place). Rising unemployment and the sharp lift in underemployment over recent
months both suggest labour market slack is building steadily.
Unlike previous readings which implied a sudden loss of momentum, employment growth now looks to

have been steadier over the first half of the year, albeit with significant month-to-month volatility. This is
in line with our belief that any further softening was more likely to occur in the second half once the
drag from higher inflation and recent interest rate rises works through the economy.
The Q2 Westpac Business Signal showcased that cost pressures are growing and consumer-facing
industries are most exposed to deteriorating growth prospects. Together with weaker employment

intentions from other business surveys, this points to a sluggish outlook for employment through the
turn of the year when we expect the unemployment rate to peak around 5.0%.
Offshore, the Middle East remained the market’s focus as tensions continued to ratchet higher.
Overnight, President Trump stated he was close to deciding on a "massive attack" against Iran and
threatened "major military punishment" for the Houthis and Iran if the Houthis attack any other ships in

the Middle East after they targeted two Saudi ships a day earlier. This follows yesterday’s threat that
US forces “will bomb and destroy ONE BRIDGE OR POWER PLANT, including those located next to,
or in, the Capital City of Tehran” any time Iran’s military “shoots at a ship in the Strait of Hormuz”. Iran
continues to hold to their position on retaliation, with like-for-like strikes on regional military installations
and infrastructure threatened. Brent oil reached USD102 overnight and currently trades just under that

level.
Fresh for markets today are the potential implications of the imposition of an announced 10% to 12.5%
tariff by the US on 99% of its imports effective 12:01am Friday Washington time. The basis for the tariff
is an assessment by US authorities of 60 countries’ restrictions on the use of forced labour in their
production chain, though the tariff catches exporters to the US to almost the same degree. This
measure replaces the temporary 10% tariff set to expire at 12:00am. As a result, it should have little-to-

no net impact on US inflation or growth. That said, it maintains pressure on US households and
businesses at a particularly inopportune time and so is a threat to sentiment.
Against that backdrop, the ECB left rates unchanged while emphasising the full inflationary impact of
higher energy prices are yet to be felt and that risks remain skewed to the upside. While June inflation
surprised to the downside and the ECB, as yet, has seen no evidence of secondary effects from 2026’s

energy price spike, heightened uncertainty and the skew of risks favour another hike in September
along with the possibility of another late in the year, if conditions do not improve.
Turning to the UK, attention centred on the first week of UK Prime Minister Andy Burnham's
government. Aside from cabinet appointments, Burnham indicated he would operate within existing
fiscal rules while pursuing a "new economic model" aimed at boosting growth, likely through cost-of-

living measures. He has already removed VAT from household electricity bills and is expected to
announce initiatives targeting youth employment and further education. Markets are anticipating a
generous Budget in November, although higher-than-expected borrowing is likely to constrain

### Page 3

Related articles
policymakers.
Economic data recently received for the UK is broadly reassuring. Labour market indicators pointed to
stabilisation, the unemployment rate holding at 4.9% in the three months to May and employment rising
by 148k. That said, alternative measures, including the Bank of England's Decision Maker Panel,
suggest the labour market is at risk of weakening further. The latest inflation data is consistent with a
gradual easing in domestic pressures. Consumer prices rose 0.1% in June, slowing annual inflation

from 2.8%yr to 2.6%yr. Services inflation was little changed at 3.6%yr, however, indicating that
underlying price pressures remain. Taken together, the data did little to materially alter expectations for
Bank of England policy.

### Page 2

在 AI 算⼒需求持续升温之际， CoreWeave 不仅交出营收翻倍的季度成绩单，还在电话会上释放
了更重要的信号 ——算⼒供给仍然紧张，价格、利润率和⽼⼀代 GPU 的商业价值都在上升。
美国当地时间周⼆， CoreWeave 公布2026 年第⼆季度财报并召开业绩电话会。公司第⼆季度营
收达到 26 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 112% 超预期；营收积压升⾄ 1040 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 46% ，且在第三
季度初⼜新增超过 250 亿美元的净新增客⼾承诺。
财报公布后， CoreWeave 美股盘后股价⼀度上涨 16% 。公司营收创下季度新⾼， AI 算⼒需求继
续推动业绩增⻓，但市场同时关注其巨额资本开⽀、亏损以及基础设施建设和供应链挑战。
相⽐财报本⾝，电话会释放出的⼏个变化更值得市场关注：
公司开始明显感受到规模带来的利润率改善； 7 ⽉已将各 SKU 价格平均上调约 25% ；托管推理业
务 ARR 从 100 万美元迅速增⾄超过 1 亿美元；与此同时，旧⼀代 GPU 依然能够以较⾼价格获得新

的合同。
“ 需求持续超过供应 ” ，新签合同利润率提⾼ 5 ⾄ 10 个百分点
CoreWeave CEO Michael Intrator 在电话会上直⾔，公司⽬前⾯对的不是需求不⾜，⽽是供给
跟不上需求。
他表⽰，随着 AI 市场从前沿模型实验室向软件、⼯业系统、⾦融市场、企业⼯作流以及国家安全
等领域扩展，需求正在变得更加⼴泛。
更重要的是，需求扩张已经开始反映在价格和利润率上。
Intrator 称，CoreWeave 第⼆季度签署的客⼾合同，其贡献利润率预计⽐最近⼏个季度签署的合
同⾼出 5 ⾄ 10 个百分点。CFO Nitin Agrawal 进⼀步表⽰，公司利润率的改善甚⾄发⽣在 7 ⽉份涨
价之前。
CoreWeave 已经在 7 ⽉份将各 SKU 价格提⾼约 25% 。公司称，此次调整既是为了应对当前需求环
境和组件成本上涨，也是因为客⼾在从训练转向推理后，从 CoreWeave 平台投资中获得的回报
正在提⾼。
CEO 解释称，利润率改善来⾃多重因素，包括 CoreWeave 基础设施的价值、可靠性、安全性和

总体拥有成本，以及客⼾⾃⾝开始通过 AI 产品实现商业化。
全年营收指引上调
强劲需求推动 CoreWeave 再次上调全年业绩指引。公司预计：
2026 年营收： 124 亿⾄ 132 亿美元，此前指引低于这⼀⽔平；
“我们的近期产能实际上已售罄。”
“很多客⼾正在将其产品货币化，因此他们更积极地介⼊，并愿意⽀付更⾼的利润率，因为他们
需要获得能够帮助他们成功的计算能⼒。”

### Page 3

调整后营业收⼊： 9.6 亿⾄ 11.5 亿美元；
全年资本开⽀： 350 亿⾄ 390 亿美元；
年底活跃电⼒：超过 1.85 吉⽡；
年底年化运⾏率营收： 185 亿⾄ 195 亿美元。
第三季度，公司预计营收达到34.5 亿⾄ 36 亿美元，调整后营业收⼊为 2 亿⾄ 2.6 亿美元，资本开
⽀则达到115 亿⾄ 135 亿美元。公司预计调整后营业利润率将在第三季度继续环⽐提升，并在第
四季度达到 “ 低两位数 ” 。
这意味着 CoreWeave 正在进⼊⼀个关键阶段：收⼊增⻓和利润率开始同时改善，但资本投⼊也
在快速膨胀。
第⼆季度资本开⽀达到 94 亿美元，在建⼯程从⼀季度的 96 亿美元升⾄ 119 亿美元。与此同时，公
司第⼆季度净亏损达到6.26 亿美元，⾼于上年同期的 2.9 亿美元；利息⽀出则从 2.67 亿美元升⾄
6.4 亿美元。
也就是说， CoreWeave 的 AI 基础设施扩张仍然⾼度依赖资本市场。
托管推理爆发： ARR 数⽉从 100 万美元增⾄超 1 亿美元

电话会释放的另⼀个重要信号，是 CoreWeave 正在从传统的⻓期 GPU 算⼒租赁，进⼀步进⼊ AI
推理市场。
公司表⽰，托管推理平台推出⼏个⽉以来，预订 ARR 已经从100 万美元增⻓⾄超过 1 亿美元，并
预计到 2026 年底⾄少达到2.5 亿美元。
Intrator 表⽰， CoreWeave 此前主要依靠⻓期 “ 照付不议 ” 合同实现快速扩张，但随着公司规模扩
⼤，需要提供更多能够产⽣更⾼利润率的软件和服务。
这也是 CoreWeave 正在重新定义⽼⼀代 GPU 价值的原因。 CFO 称，即将进⼊续约期的旧⼀代产
品只是公司机群中 “ ⾮常有限的⼀部分 ” ，但这些 GPU 的 ASP 仍然较⾼，基本处于⼀年前的⽔平。
更值得关注的是，公司已经证明⽼ GPU 仍然能够获得⻓期合同。 Agrawal 举例称， CoreWeave
最近签署了⼀份 A100 合同，价格具有吸引⼒，并且合同期限延续⾄2029 年。 A100 这⼀ GPU 架
构最早于 2020 年推出。
Intrator 对此表⽰：

“我们确实认为，这为我们提供了⼀个绝佳的机会，不仅提供最前沿的计算能⼒，也是⼀种很好
地利⽤即将到期的合同中的 GPU ，为公司⻓期提取最⼤价值的⽅式。”
“我们已经能够将⼀款架构可追溯到 2020 年的 GPU ，在⼀份完全定价⾄ 2029 年的合同中售出。”

### Page 4

对于市场⽽⾔，这意味着随着 AI 算⼒需求持续增⻓，GPU 的商业⽣命周期可能⽐此前预期更
⻓。即使新⼀代芯⽚不断推出，旧 GPU 仍可能被⽤于推理、量化交易以及其他对最先进算⼒要
求较低的⼯作负载。
AI 从 “ 训练⼀次 ” 变成持续消耗算⼒
Intrator 在电话会上反复强调的⼀个核⼼判断，是 AI 算⼒需求正在发⽣结构性变化。
过去，企业往往把模型训练视为⼀次性的项⽬：训练、部署，然后结束。但如今， AI 应⽤进⼊⽣
产环境后，训练、推理、评估和改进形成了⼀个持续循环。
这也是 CoreWeave 试图扩⼤软件、托管推理、开发⼯具和编排服务的原因。公司表⽰，⽬前平
台上被跟踪的模型训练运⾏次数已经超过10 亿次； GPU 之外的存储、 CPU 、⽹络和软件等业务
ARR 已经超过4 亿美元。
CoreWeave 希望从⼀家单纯提供 GPU 算⼒的公司，转型为覆盖 AI 完整⽣命周期的基础设施平
台。如果这⼀模式成⽴，公司未来的增⻓空间将不再完全取决于新增 GPU 数量，⽽取决于 AI 应

⽤从训练⾛向⽣产、推理以及持续迭代之后产⽣的⻓期计算需求。
对于市场⽽⾔， CoreWeave 此次电话会释放的重要的信号是 ——需求仍然强劲，价格正在上
涨，旧 GPU 仍有价值，利润率开始改善，但为了满⾜这轮需求，公司也必须继续以极⾼的资本
强度扩张。
CoreWeave 2026 年第⼆季度财报电话会议全⽂翻译如下：
“计算不再是在模型⽣命周期开始时集中的⼀次性需求。它变成了⼀种持续的需求，随着⽣产中
的每个应⽤程序和每个改进周期⽽增⻓。”
Michael Intrator ，⾸席执⾏官：
⼤家下午好，感谢各位的参与。第⼆季度对  CoreWeave 来说是出⾊的⼀個季度。我们在各⽅⾯
都超出了计划，我们⼀直在构建的运营杠杆开始在我们的业绩中清晰地显现出来。整个组织⾮凡
的执⾏⼒推动了创纪录的财务表现、快速的产能增⻓、不断扩⼤的客⼾需求以及持续的平台创
新。我们实现了创纪录的  26 亿美元营收，同⽐增⻓  112% ，营收积压增⻓⾄  1040 亿美元，同
时推动了企业应⽤的快速扩张。

这⼀数字不包括我们在第三季度初⼏周内新增的超过  250 亿美元的净新增客⼾承诺。我们继续
执⾏我们的电⼒战略，活跃电⼒达到  1.5 吉⽡，新增近  500 兆⽡，超过了我们历史上任何⼀个
季度的增⻓，并且同⽐增⻑了两倍多。我们仍然坚定地⾛在到  2030 年实现⾄少  8 吉⽡⽬标的轨
道上。我们将调整后营业收⼊增⾄  1.28 亿美元，利润率显著扩⼤，因为我们的规模⽇益转化为
运营杠杆，并且我们继续拓宽我们的技术栈，交付了七项新的  AI 平台功能，并实现了多项⾏业
⾸创⾥程碑，使客⼾能够更快、更⼤规模地构建、部署和运营  AI 。
我们取得的惊⼈进步是整个组织的证明，业务也变得越来越强⼤。在第⼆季度，我们签署的客⼾
合同带来的贡献利润率预计将⽐最近⼏个季度签署的合同⾼出  5 到  10 个百分点。这不仅仅是⼀
系列⾥程碑，它证明了  AI 市场的发展符合我们建⽴  CoreWeave 时所依据的信念。我们相信  AI
时代已经到来，并最终将触及全球经济的每⼀个部分。每个组织都在被转型，这创造了重塑既有

市场和创造全新市场的机会。未来将由那些抓住机遇的先驱者引领，⽆论是处于前沿的  AI 原⽣
公司，还是成熟企业内部变⾰的推动者。以光速学习和迭代现在已成为  AI 领导⼒的基本要求。

### Page 5

我们相信这些先驱者需要⼀种新型平台来⼤规模释放  AI 的潜⼒。这些信念是驱动我们战略的运
营假设。它们指导着我们的产品路线图、资本配置、合作伙伴关系，并最终指导我们如何服务客
⼾。今天，我想讨论我们的愿景如何在四个领域得到体现：第⼀，不断扩⼤的需求和客⼾采⽤；
第⼆，我们在平台上提供的持续  AI 开发⼯具；第三，我们的  AI 原⽣架构所带来的性能和经济
效益；第四，⽀撑多年增⻓的电⼒和供应链基础。 AI 正在改变每⼀个组织。关于未来  AI 云基础
设施需求的争论可能会继续，但我们确切知道的是，这些信息来⾃于我们客⼾的⾏动。
随着市场在⾏业、地域、⼯作负载和  GPU 架构代际上的拓展，需求持续增强。 AI 不再局限于前
沿模型实验室。它正在嵌⼊软件、⼯业系统、⾦融市场、企业⼯作流和国家安全任务中。我们在
积压订单、签署的新承诺以及整个平台的利⽤率和定价环境中都看到了这种⼴度。我们的
Blackwell 和  Vera Rubin SKU 的定价和利润率创下新⾼，⽽前⼏代  SKU 的定价与⼏年前持平

或更⾼。我们的近期产能实际上已售罄。这正转化为来⾃更⼴泛客⼾群的、条款⽇益有利的已签
署承诺，并使  CoreWeave 能够在未来数年获得市场份额。每个组织都⾯临着重新思考可能性的
挑战。
AI 不仅仅是加速现有流程。它使企业能够重新设计核⼼功能，创建新产品，并进⼊以前不存在
的市场。 Caterpillar 是⼀个有⼒的例⼦。我们将共同部署  NVIDIA 的  Vera Rubin 平台，以⽀持
Caterpillar 在⼯业规模上的物理  AI 训练和推理。 Caterpillar 将使⽤  AI 云基础设施作为其数据⼯
⼚，训练专⻔的模型，以增强⾃主施⼯设备的智能性和⽣产⼒。⽣命科学正在成为  CoreWeave
的另⼀个重要增⻓领域。因为应对世界上⼀些最复杂科学挑战的组织越来越多地转向我们的平
台。我们最近迎来了  Isomorphic Labs 作为新客⼾，并很⾼兴能⽀持他们解决所有疾病的使命。
⾦融服务也是我们的⼀个主要增⻓领域。我们最近将  Flow Traders 和  IMC 加⼊了我们不断壮⼤

的系统化交易公司名单。这些客⼾正在使⽤  CoreWeave 开发和部署⽤于量化交易的下⼀代  AI
模型。他们选择我们的平台是因为我们能够以这些应⽤所需的速度、可靠性和效率来编排⾼性能
⼯作负载。在公共部⻔，我们与  Leidos 的合作标志着  CoreWeave Federal 增⻓的重要⼀步。
我们正在共同努⼒，加速为国防、国家安全和情报任务提供安全的  AI 能⼒。 AI 的未来正由新⼀
代创新者构建。有些是在前沿运营的  AI 原⽣公司，另⼀些则是愿意挑战现状的成熟企业内部变
⾰推动者。
CoreWeave 为两者提供服务。像  Descartes 这样的公司正在使⽤我们的平台开发  Oasis 3 ，这
是⾸个可通过  API 访问的物理  AI 世界模型。 IBM 正在使⽤  CoreWeave 安全地试验强化学习、
代理⼯具使⽤和模型评估。通过  Monolith ，我们的专业现场⼯程师直接与  Nissan 和  ZF 等客⼾
合作，加速企业级  AI 应⽤的开发。需求现在也扩展到了我们的基础设施之外。通过

CoreWeave Omni ，我们看到了来⾃主权国家、企业和云客⼾的浓厚兴趣。在过去的⼏周⾥，我
们签署了第⼀笔交易，该交易将于  2027 年开始扩展。这些例⼦因⾏业和⽤例⽽异，但模式是⼀
致的。
AI 正从实验阶段进⼊核⼼运营阶段，果断⾏动的组织正在创造优势。部署不再是终点线。随着
AI 进⼊⽣产环境，应⽤程序的构建⽅式正在发⽣变化，领导者将是那些学习和迭代最快的⼈。
在过去的⼏年⾥，许多组织将模型视为可交付成果：训练它，部署它，然后继续前进。企业不再
那样运作了。训练、推理、评估和改进现在形成了⼀个单⼀的连续循环。⽣产中的模型和代理⽣
成现实世界的数据。这些数据为评估提供信息，推动新的实验，从⽽在重新部署到⽣产环境之前
改进模型或应⽤程序。这个循环重复进⾏，能⼒随时间复合增⻓。这种转变从根本上改变了需求
曲线和  AI 的经济性。
计算不再是在模型⽣命周期开始时集中的⼀次性需求。它变成了⼀种持续的需求，随着⽣产中的
每个应⽤程序和每个改进周期⽽增⻓。我们的  AI 原⽣平台正是为此⽽构建的。它涵盖了尖端的

云基础设施、快速增⻓的托管推理业务、领先的开发⼯具和代理解决⽅案，以及由  Mission
Control ⽀持的同类最佳编排和可观测性层。这些功能共同为客⼾提供了⼀个集成环境。
客⼾通过  CoreWeave Inference 部署应⽤程序，使⽤我们的模型或他们通过我们的⽆服务器功
能定制的模型，使⽤  Weights & Biases 监控性能，在⽣产中评估应⽤程序，试验新模型，通过

⽆服务器强化学习或沙盒优化性能，并在将任何更改返回⽣产之前，根据质量、性能和成本进⾏

⼀个实验到⽣产改进之间的时间。在第⼆季度，我们推出了七项新的  AI 平台功能，并实现了多

这些创新是与我们的客⼾和合作伙伴共同开发的，旨在解决实际的⽣产挑战。这就是为什么我们
看到了如此强劲的采⽤。就在本周，我们平台上跟踪的模型训练运⾏次数超过了  10 亿次。这个
数字背后是数百万次实验、数千项研究突破以及⼀个不断壮⼤的⼯程师、研究⼈员和组织社区，
他们正在构建下⼀代  AI 。我们的  AI 开发服务具有更⾼的利润率，并且被⽐我们的核⼼云更⼴泛
的客⼾群所采⽤。这被证明是⼀条⾃然的客⼾扩展路径，因为今天构建  AI 应⽤的开发者就是明
天  AI 云基础设施的客⼾。通过及早为他们服务，我们正在建⽴随着  AI ⼯作负载扩展⽽⾃然扩

编码代理，服务他们⾃⼰的微调模型，并⼤规模部署开放权重模型。客⼾不应该为了速度⽽牺牲
成本。在  CoreWeave ，他们不必如此。他们选择我们的平台是因为我们的总体拥有成本、质

Max M3 等开源模型，我们在每个代币成本和⾸字响应时间⽅⾯持续领先。⽽这种领先地位正直
接转化为收⼊。⾃推出以来的⼏个⽉⾥，我们托管推理平台的预订  ARR 已从  100 万美元增⻓到
超过  1 亿美元。我们预计到  2026 年底，托管推理  ARR ⾄少达到  2.5 亿美元。先驱者需要⼀种

不同的平台。持续的  AI ⽣命周期⽆法通过简单地将  GPU 添加到通⽤云中来⽀持。它需要⼀种

⽀出的资产级债务，同时产⽣了⼤量额外的⾃由现⾦流。因此，当初始合同结束时，该集群不再

## Document 322: Diversification_ Why bonds no longer hedge equities

Source PDF: all report pdf/Diversification_ Why bonds no longer hedge equities.pdf
Report source: Allianz Trade
Date: 2026-07-28
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

In 
Summary  
• 
 The 60/40 portfolio has quietly stopped working.  
The risk
 -
adjusted return investors 
could reasonably expect from the standard balanced portfolio has fallen to roughly a 
third of its long
 -
run level, 
from a Sharpe ratio of 0.47 to just 0.14, 
 as bonds and equities 
have stopped hedging each other for the first time in a generation. Since mid
 -
2022
 , 
the two have moved together rather than apart, 
 with the equity
 -
bond correlation 
flipping to a positive +0.77, 
 and no comparable episode in the past quarter
 -
century 
has behaved this way for anywhere near this long.  
• 
 The culprit is policy uncertainty, not  
only  
inflation.  
The reassuring story that 2022 
was a one
 -
off inflation shock and that everything returns to normal once prices settle 
does not survive contact with the data. Inflation still matters, but it is no longer the 
master variable. Tariff policy, geopolitical fra
 gmentation and the slow erosion of 
central  
bank credibility are now doing the heavy lifting.  
• 
 Gold, private credit and infrastructure are the diversifiers doing the work now.  
Where government bonds have failed to catch equity drawdowns since 2022, three 
asset classes have quietly stepped into their place. Gold has delivered positive risk
 -
adjusted returns in nearly every regime tested and is the closest thing to an all
 -
weather  
hedge
 . Private credit is posting its strongest showing on record. Infrastructure 
has held up across both this cycle and calmer ones before it, a rare cross
 -
regime 
performer.  
• 
 A regime
 -
robust 'No
 -
Regret' portfolio
 , 
 built to work across cycles rather than 
optimized for the current one,  
 roughly doubles the risk
 -
adjusted return of a 
standard global benchmark
 . 
 It 
 delivers a Sharpe ratio of 1.
 33 
 against the 
benchmark's 0.5
 9
, compounds an initial dollar to roughly 9x its starting value over two 
decades against just 5x for the benchmark and cuts the deepest drawdown in the 
sample from −24.6% to −1
 5
.
3
%
. The composition 
 marks a substantial departure from 
tradition: government bonds are cut from roughly 20% of the benchmark to under 
 4
% 
and public equities from 43% to 1
 3
%, funding materially larger allocations to gold 
(1
6
%), private credit (2
 9
%) and private equity (
 16
%), with a smaller residual position in 
infrastructure
 . 
• 
 The 
 historical 
 hedge 
 will 
 return only once policy uncertainty resolves and inflation 
is fully re
 -
anchored, and rate cuts alone will not do it.  
 Only two of the five 
preconditions for a durable return to negative equity
 -
bond correlation are true 
binding constraints, and neither is something a central bank can deliver on its own. 
Genuine resolution likely requires a durable Iran settlement and a mo
 re stable US 
trade regime, 
 and 
 both 
 look like multi
 y
ear propositions rather than near  
term fixes
 . 
The remaining condit
 ions, on monetary and liquidity settings, equity valuations and 
the yield curve, follow mechanically once the first two are in place.

How 2022 snapped t
 he 60/40 
 strategy   
For two decades, the 60/40 portfolio did something rather remarkable
 : 
it made the difficult look dull. 
 The formula 
was straightforward. Sixty cents of every dollar bought equities, which supplied the long
 -
run growth. The other  
40 
went into government bonds, which stood by, patiently, waiting to catch the fall. When equities dropped, as they 
periodically did, bonds duly rose. Growth scares brought rate cuts, rate cuts pushed bond prices up and the 40% 
sleeve absorbed a decent share  
of the equity pain. Invest
 ors could speak, without embarrassment, of a portfolio 
that returned around 
 7% 
a year with the volatility of a mid
 -
cap stock and the drama of an accountancy conference.   
But i
 t was a convention, not a law of nature.  
 Yet it held often enough, and for long enough, that the entire 
architecture of modern retirement finance came to be built on top of it. Liability
 -
matched pension books assumed 
it. Target
 -
date funds 
 mechanized  
it. An industry of insurance companies, endowments and family offices arranged 
themselves around the same quiet promise
 , 
that bonds would zig when equities zagged.  
Then, in 2022, the convention snapped  
a
nd the wound is still open.  
Between the early 2000s and 2021, a 60/40 
book delivered a Sharpe ratio of roughly 0.47
 , a measure of how much excess return a portfolio earns per unit of 
risk taken (above 0.5 is respectable, above 1.0 enviable). It was  
 unglamorous, but the sort of number pension 
trustees could take to their boards without flinching. In 2022, the hedge failed at the moment it was most needed. 
Equities and bonds sold off in unison, and the balanced portfolio delivered its deepest drawdown  
in a generation. 
A 
 manager  
reviewing the year's returns could be forgiven for a moment of vertigo
 : 
the diversification premium, 
dependable for so long that it had ceased to feel like a premium at all, had simply vanished.   
Four years on, markets have not forgotten.  
The Sharpe ratio implied by today's pricing on a 60/40 book sits closer 
to 0.14, less than a third of its long
 -
run average. Portfolios that had been sold, quite reasonably, as boring have 
been forced to justify themselves anew  
(Figure 1)
 . 
Figure 1: 
 When bonds stopped catching the fall  
(%)

Sources: 
 LSEG Datastream, Allianz Research

At this point t
 he industry has, predictably, split into two camps.  
The optimists take a relatively benign view
 . 
2022 
was an inflation shock and inflation shocks have always played havoc with the correlation between bonds and 
equities. Once inflation settles, and provided the world manages to avoid a second, 1970s
 -
style episode, the old 
negative correlation should q
 uietly reassert itself, as ordinary growth shocks reclaim the driver's seat. On this 
reading, patience is the only strategy required.  
The pessimists point to something rather harder to unwind. Fiscal 
and monetary policy have become, if not fused, then unco
 mfortably entangled. Geopolitical fragmentation is 
running at levels not seen since the end of the cold war. And central  
bank credibility, that intangible asset which 
underwrote so much of the previous cycle, has been slowly, publicly, corroded. On this view, the positive correlation

### Page 3

3

can persist long after inflation itself has been tamed, because what has changed is not the price level but the 
framework around it.   
In this paper we  
apply 
 both 
theories 
 to post
 -
2022 data and  
find that  
the pessimists have the better of it, though 
with an important wrinkle.  
The dominant force behind today's positive correlation is not the monthly inflation print, 
awaited each month with such nervous attention on Wall Street, but policy uncertainty itself. Inflation still matters, 
of course. It shows up in the regression
 , 
 alongside tighter liquidity, a flatter yield curve and stretched equity 
valuations, as a genuine driver of correlation. It is simply not the master variable.  
The implication is uncomforta
 ble, 
particularly for those who have spent the past two years waiting for a single, clean catalyst. A first rate cut, however 
loudly cheered, will not on its own restore the hedge. Policy uncertainty is the binding constraint, and the bar for its 
removal i
 s a good deal higher than markets are currently pricing. Investors counting on a return to the old regime  
may find themselves counting for some time.  
Where are we in the cycle?  
Five regimes, not one endless cycle. 
 The temptation, when confronted with the current mess, is to reach for the 
nearest historical analogue and declare it a rerun of the 1970s. This is almost always a mistake. Instead of assuming 
the past 
 25 
years amount to a single continuous cycle, we sifted the macro data into five distinct regimes, each with 
its own equity
 -
bond correlation and, if one is honest, its own personality.  
The first, which we call liquidity and rates 
normalization
 , ran from 2001 to 2008 and produced the workhorse negative correlation of 
 -
0.38
, 
not spectacular, 
but exactly the diversification investors had learned to expect.  
(Correlation runs from minus one, a perfect mirror 
image, through zero, no relationship at all, to plus one
 , perfect lockstep; the more negative the number, the more 
reliably bonds cushion equity losses.)  
Crisis and deleveraging, the label for the acute shocks of 2008
 -
09 and 2020, 
was brief but violent, and pushed the correlation to 
 -
0.69, the strongest hedge in the entire sample; when the world 
briefly ended, in other words, bonds worked harder than ever. Post
 -
crisis reflation, the recovery years of 2009 to 
2012, offered a more modest 
 -
0.19, with wide credit spreads and a steep yield curve. Then came the long r
 isk 
on 
expansion of 2012 to 2022, a decade in which valuations rose, volatility slept and correlations grew lazy, flattening 
the hedge to 
 -
0.12.  
And then, in May 2022, the arrangement changed. 
 Today's regime  
is the odd one out. Elevated rates, stubborn 
inflation, high policy uncertainty and a compressed yield curve have combined to push the equity
 -
bond correlation 
to a 
+
0.77, a figure without precedent anywhere in the past 
 25 
years
 . 
Overlay the five regimes onto 
 realized  
 equity returns and the rolling bond
 -
equity correlation and a familiar 
picture emerges
 : 
T
he hedge holds through every "normal" regime and breaks only when inflation becomes the 
dominant shock.  
Through the dot
 -
com bust and the subsequent recovery of 2003 to 2007, 
 the 
correlation stays 
firmly negative, touching 
 -
60% even as equity returns swing from 
 -
40% to 
 +
40%. Rates and the shape of the curve 
did most of the work. The Fed entered 2001 with the funds rate still at 6.5%,  
cut aggressively through the dot
 -
com 
bust to 1.75% by year
 -
end and, because short rates fell faster than long ones, watched the curve steepen sharply 
even as growth expectations collapsed. That combination, elevated starting rates giving the Fed room to c
 ut and a 
yield curve that could steepen on cue, is precisely the mechanism that lets bonds rally when equities sell off. It is, in 
other words, a textbook illustration of why bonds do their job as shock absorber at exactly the moment equities 
need them to.  
Then came 2008. The global financial crisis was, above all, a credit and volatility episode
 . 
T
hose two forces 
dominated all other drivers, sentiment and growth expectations collapsed to sample lows and the recovery of 2009 
and 2010 was a sharp, V
 -
shaped affair driven by quantitative easing, which briefly, and unusually, pushed both 
equities and cor
 relation higher together. What followed was the decade
 -
long 
 stabilization  
regime of 2011 to 2020, 
in which correlation settled into a shallow, range
 -
bound negativ
 e
-
to
-
neutral band and equity returns unfolded in 
the calmer, low
 -
rate manner that came to define the era. The C
 ovid
-
19 
shock of 2020 was narrow and sharp, marked 
by the highest readings in the sample for monetary and liquidity conditions as central banks intervened at 
unprecedented scale. It, too, resolved quickly.

### Page 4

4

The regime that breaks this pattern is defined by policy, not growth, and that is precisely why we do not expect 
it to resolve on its own.  
Since 2022, the correlation between bonds and equities has decoupled from its historical 
negative range and climbed into the 60% to 80% band, even as equity returns themselves have been comparatively 
muted. Unlike 2001, the Fed entered this shock with rat
 es near zero, and there was, therefore, no room to cut when 
inflation and growth diverged. Instead, consumer  
 price inf
 lation and commodity inflation reached their highest 
levels in the sample, the dollar strengthened, equity valuations sold off, and hiking rather than cutting pushed bonds 
and equities in the same direction.  
By 2024
 , 
the character of the shock had rotated. Inflation itself cooled, but policy uncertainty and interest  
rate 
levels took over as the elevated forces, while monetary and liquidity conditions sank to sample lows. 
 Tariff policy 
and geopolitical shocks now sustain the same positive
 -
correlation dynamic that inflation started. This, more than 
anything, is why the current regime should be treated as structurally different, not merely a noisier version of the 
stabilization  
years that preceded it  
(Figure 2)
 . 
Figure 
 2
:

 -
century in five acts

The current regime is structurally different, not simply unusually long.  
Three features set it apart from every prior 
episode. The first is duration and consistency
 , 
every single quarter since May 2022 has fallen into this regime, and 
the average correlation has stayed stubbornly above 
 +
0.40 throughout. There is no precedent in the sample for that 
kind of persistence. The second is the identity of the dominant signal. It is not merely inflation, as many assumed at 
the outset, but policy uncertainty specifically. Our Economic Policy Uncert
 ainty index, expressed as a z
 -
score (tha
 t 
is, in standard deviations away from its long
 -
run average, so that 
 +
1 means one standard deviation above trend 
and anything above 
 +
2 is unusually high), sits at 
 +
1.7 as of May 2026. It has been elevated since 2024, and briefly 
touched a remarkable 
 +
5.4 on 
“L
iberation 
 D
ay
 ”
. The third, most awkward of the three is that this regime has never 
ended. Every quarter since May 2022 has remained inside it, with no observed exit anywhere in the historical record.  
There are two plausible exits from here, and one rather less likely one.  
A transition into the risk  
on expansion 
regime, in which policy uncertainty resolves and liquidity conditions ease, is one credible route; a transition into the 
liquidity
 -
normalization  
regime, in which rates settle into a predictable path and broad liquidity returns to the system, 
is the other. Both are structurally consistent with the way prior regimes have unwound. A direct jump into an acute 
crisis regime is also possible, though fa
 r less probable, and historically brief. Those episodes have, on average, 
lasted only around five months before resolving into something calmer.

What is driving the  
change in correlation? 
 An a
 natomy of a broken hedge  
Policy uncertainty is the biggest driver, but inflation matters too. 
 To work out which macro forces have actually 
been moving the equity
 -
bond correlation, we regressed 
 25 
years of rolling correlation data against five candidate 
factors. The results are unambiguous. Roughly two
 -
thirds of the variation in correlation can be traced to those five 
drivers, and all five clear the usual statistical thresholds comfortably.  
Policy uncertainty emerges as the dominant 
force, pushing correlation firmly into positive te
 rritory whenever it rises. Monetary and liquidity conditions carry an 
almost identical, but opposite, effect
 , 
looser conditions restore the traditional hedge, tighter ones destroy it. Equity 
valuations turn out to matter more than most would guess
 . 
A 
stretched market pushes correlation up, a calmer one 
pulls it back down. A steeper yield curve, the mark of a "normal" rate environment, works quietly on the negative 
side. And inflation, contrary to those hoping the story is a pure policy
 -
uncertainty mor
 ality 
 play, also pushes 
correlation up in its own right, an effect that has been especially pronounced since 2022.  
That inflation still matters is a corrective to the pure policy  
uncertainty story.  
The two forces have moved together, 
often, over the past two decades. In 2008 and 2020, the monetary and liquidity indicator surged to nearly 3.8 
standard deviations above its long
 -
run average, and correlation briefly flipped positive alongside extreme ea
 sing. 
In 2021, correlation turned positive ahead of the inflation shock itself
 . 
T
he two then rose together as consumer prices 
climbed, with CPI reaching 3.9 standard deviations above trend in 2022 while correlation
 's z
-
score 
ran up to a peak 
near 2. The po
 st
-
2021 regime, in other words, was inflation
 -
driven before it became uncertainty
 -
driven. 
The character of the shock rotated after that.  
In 2022, monetary and liquidity conditions collapsed to almost 2.8 
standard deviations below trend as the Fed hiked, yet correlation stayed elevated: growth was no longer the master 
shock, inflation had taken over. Inflation then receded faster than corre
 lation, which anchored near 1.5 to 2 through 
2024 and 2025 as policy uncertainty stepped in as the marginal driver, consistent with the tariff
 -
driven uncertainty 
of the second Trump administration. The sharpest  
reading in the sample, policy uncertainty touching 5
 .4 
in early 
2025, captures the 
 “L
iberation 
 D
ay
 ” 
shock. Uncertainty then remained stubbornly high through the escalation to 
"major combat operations" in the February 2026 Iran conflict, a longer and more consequential episode than the 
brief Israel
 -
Iran strikes of June 2025 (which had only pushed Brent c
 rude from around 
 USD
 65 to the low 
 USD
 80s 
before subsiding). All of it feeds the same inflation
 -
and
 -
uncertainty channel that has kept bonds and equities 
correlated near their sample highs.  
The model, reassuringly, still works. 
 Its predictions match reality most closely when one or two forces dominate 
the regime, and most poorly when short, sharp shocks temporarily overwhelm the underlying macro drivers. The fit 
is at its best during the 2001 to 2008 and 2022 to 2026 windows, pre
 cisely when a small number of factors have 
been doing most of the driving
 , 
rates and the yield curve in the early 2000s, and policy uncertainty and monetary 
policy together from 2022 onwards. It tracks worst around the  
2009 to 2010 rebound and the 2020 C
 ovid
 -
19 
dip, 
when actual correlation overshot the model in both directions faster than macro variables could move. Those, 
unsurprisingly, are precisely the moments when positioning, liquidity dislocations and forced deleveraging drive 
realized  
co
-
movement independent of the fundamentals the model measures. The gap widens for a while, then 
both series converge again.  
Feeding May 2026 inputs into the model produces a predicted correlation reading of 
1.05 standard deviations  
above the long
 -
run average, against an actual reading of 1.59. Policy uncertainty is the 
largest contributor at 
 +
0.62, with equity valuations, monetary and liquidity conditions and inflation each 
contributing between 
 +
0.11 and 
 +
0.14, and a re
 -
steepening yield curve chipping in a marginal 
 +
0.04. The gap of 
+
0.54 between prediction and reality is larger than in prior months, consistent with a degree of regime persistence 
the underlying factors alone do not capture. The correlation has become, in effect, self
 -
reinforcing
 . 
 P
ortfolio 
managers have already cut their duration exposure in response to it, and their reduced participation keeps 
 realized  
co
-
movement elevated even as the underlying drivers ease a fraction  
(Figure 3 and 4)
 .

What has to happen before the hedge normalizes?  
A durable return to negative bond
 -
equity correlation requires 
five conditions to line up. 
 The good news is that only two of them are genuine constraints. The other three are 
largely endogenous to a normal easing cycle and should follow, mechanically, once it is underway.  
The hard ones first. Policy uncertainty must recede, and inflation must remain on a credible disinflation path. 
Neither is something a central bank can deliver on its own, which is why we do not expect the hedge to return simply 
because the Fed or the ECB begin cutting. Policy uncertainty today is anchored by an unresolved geopolitical and 
trade backdrop, with the I
 ran conflict's ceasefire collapsing again in July 2026 and tariff policy still being litigated 
through the courts and alternative executive auth
 orities. Genuine resolution likely requires a durable Iran 
settlement and a more stable US trade regime, 
 and 
 both 
 look like multi
 -
year propositions rather than near
 -
term 
fixes
 . Inflation, for its part, has retraced most of its 2022 surge, but tariff pass
 -
through and Gulf
 -
driven energy 
volatility keep the disinflation path only partially secured.  
The remaining three conditions should follow of their own accord.  
Monetary and liquidity conditions will loosen 
as the new Fed leadership under Kevin Warsh, 
 who was expected at his May 2026 installation to lean toward easier 
policy but has so far held a more neutral stance
 , continues to cut rates. Equity valuations, a channel of similar size 
to policy uncertainty and liquidity in the updated regression, are likely to moderate as the AI capital
 -
spending cycle 
cools or broadens into green
 -
transition and infra
 structure investment rather than through an outright shock. And 
the yield curve should steepen as a natural consequence of rates 
 normalizing  
once the first four conditions are in 
train. Investors waiting on the first cut, in short, are waiting on the wrong thing  
(Figure 5)
